    27	// D5's classifier over a runTestCommand result — exported for the Windows-safe signal test (DR-06)
    28	function classifyProbe(run) {
    29	  // run = runTestCommand's shape: { out, status, signal, error }
    30	  const { out } = run;
    31	  if (run.error) return { id: 'D5', status: 'finding', detail: `test command failed to spawn: ${run.error}`, fix: 'fix the test command' };
    32	  if (run.signal) return { id: 'D5', status: 'finding', detail: `test command was killed by signal ${run.signal}`, fix: 'fix the test command' };
    33	  const idRe = new RegExp(DEFAULT_ID);
    34	  const { results, untagged, untaggedFails, unattributedFailures, bailout, tapProblems } = parseTap(out, idRe);
    35	  if (bailout) return { id: 'D5', status: 'finding', detail: `test run aborted: ${bailout}`, fix: 'fix the test command' };
    36	  // the lexer's protocol problems (unsupported version, YAML, plan placement) are seam findings (DR-14)
    37	  if (tapProblems && tapProblems.length)
    38	    return { id: 'D5', status: 'finding', detail: `TAP protocol: ${tapProblems[0]}`, fix: 'emit supported TAP (12/13/14 or no version line) from the test command' };
    39	  // TAP on stderr with a silent stdout: the channel is misrouted, not missing (DR-14)
    40	  if (!out.trim() && looksLikeTap(run.stderr))
    41	    return { id: 'D5', status: 'finding', detail: 'TAP arrives on stderr while stdout is silent — verify reads stdout only', fix: 'append 2>&1 to the test command' };
    42	  if (!out.trim()) return { id: 'D5', status: 'finding', detail: 'test command produced no output', fix: 'fix the test command' };
    43	  if (zeroTapParsed(out, results, untagged, unattributedFailures))
    44	    return { id: 'D5', status: 'finding', detail: 'output is not TAP — node test runner needs --test-reporter=tap', fix: 'emit TAP from the test command' };
    45	  const parsed = [...results.values()].reduce((s, r) => s + r.pass + r.fail, 0) + untagged.length + unattributedFailures.length;
    46	  const failCount = [...results.values()].reduce((s, r) => s + r.fail, 0) + untaggedFails;
    47	  // an unexplained non-zero exit is classified BEFORE any ok branch — a non-zero `1..0` run lands here
    48	  if (run.status !== null && run.status !== 0 && failCount === 0)
    49	    return { id: 'D5', status: 'finding', detail: `test command exited ${run.status} unexplained by TAP — truncated?`, fix: 'fix the test command' };
    50	  if (parsed === 0) {
    51	    if (/^1\.\.0\b/m.test(out)) return { id: 'D5', status: 'ok', detail: 'TAP plumbing OK (empty suite, 1..0)' };
    52	    return { id: 'D5', status: 'finding', detail: 'TAP stream truncated or malformed (version/plan but zero result lines)', fix: 'fix the test command' };
    53	  }
    54	  const pass = parsed - failCount;
    55	  return { id: 'D5', status: 'ok', detail: `TAP plumbing OK (${pass}p/${failCount}f — test failures are verify's business, not doctor's)` };
    56	}
    57	
    58	// The whole diagnosis. → { code, result, findings, checks, errors }
    59	function runDoctor(opts = {}) {
    60	  const cwd = opts.cwd || process.cwd();
    61	  const checks = [], errors = [];
    62	  const finish = (code) => {
    63	    const findings = checks.filter((c) => c.status === 'finding').length;
    64	    const result = code === 2 ? 'UNUSABLE' : (findings ? 'FINDINGS' : 'HEALTHY');
    65	    return { code: code !== undefined ? code : (findings ? 1 : 0), result, findings, checks, errors };
    66	  };
    67	
    68	  // D1 — Node floor (version injectable for tests, DR-12)
    69	  const major = parseInt((opts.nodeVersion || process.versions.node), 10);
    70	  if (major < NODE_FLOOR) {
    71	    checks.push({ id: 'D1', status: 'finding', detail: `Node ${opts.nodeVersion || process.versions.node} is below the supported floor (>= ${NODE_FLOOR})`, fix: 'upgrade Node' });
    72	    errors.push(`Node below the supported floor (>= ${NODE_FLOOR})`);
    73	    return finish(2);
    74	  }
    75	  checks.push({ id: 'D1', status: 'ok', detail: `Node ${opts.nodeVersion || process.versions.node} >= ${NODE_FLOOR}` });
    76	
    77	  // D2 — init scaffold
    78	  const ap = path.join(cwd, 'apriori');
    79	  if (!isDir(ap)) {
    80	    checks.push({ id: 'D2', status: 'finding', detail: `no apriori/ here — not an apriori project yet`, fix: `run 'apriori init'` });
    81	    errors.push(`no apriori/ directory — run 'apriori init'`);
    82	    return finish(2);
    83	  }
    84	  const gaps = [];
    85	  if (!isFile(path.join(ap, 'runbook.md'))) gaps.push([`apriori/runbook.md ${fs.existsSync(path.join(ap, 'runbook.md')) ? 'is not a regular file' : 'missing'}`, `apriori init`]);
    86	  if (!isDir(path.join(ap, 'specs'))) gaps.push([`apriori/specs/ ${fs.existsSync(path.join(ap, 'specs')) ? 'is not a directory' : 'missing (half-initialized scaffold)'}`, `apriori init`]);
    87	  const giText = readIfFile(path.join(ap, '.gitignore'));
    88	  if (giText === null || !/^tmp\/$/m.test(giText)) gaps.push(['apriori/.gitignore missing, unreadable, or missing its tmp/ line', `apriori update`]);
    89	  if (!isDir(path.join(ap, 'tmp'))) gaps.push([`apriori/tmp/ ${fs.existsSync(path.join(ap, 'tmp')) ? 'is not a directory' : 'dir missing'}`, `apriori update / mkdir`]);
    90	  if (gaps.length) for (const [detail, fix] of gaps) checks.push({ id: 'D2', status: 'finding', detail, fix });
    91	  else checks.push({ id: 'D2', status: 'ok', detail: 'scaffold complete' + (fs.existsSync(path.join(ap, 'process-config.md')) ? '' : ' (no process-config.md — optional, defaults apply)') });
    92	
    93	  // D3 — runbook freshness (absence OR wrong type is D2's finding; D3 must never contradict or crash)
    94	  if (!isFile(path.join(ap, 'runbook.md')))
    95	    checks.push({ id: 'D3', status: 'n/a', detail: 'runbook missing or not a regular file — see D2; freshness not checkable' });
    96	  else {
    97	    const warn = checkRunbookFreshness(cwd);
    98	    checks.push(warn.length
    99	      ? { id: 'D3', status: 'finding', detail: warn[0], fix: 'apriori update' }
   100	      : { id: 'D3', status: 'ok', detail: 'runbook copy matches the installed CLI' });
   101	  }
   102	
   103	  // D4 — tool pointers for DETECTED tools only
   104	  const detected = detectTools(cwd);
   105	  if (!detected.length) checks.push({ id: 'D4', status: 'n/a', detail: 'no known AI-tool markers detected' });
   106	  else {
   107	    let broken = 0;
   108	    for (const key of detected) {
   109	      const t = TOOLS[key];
   110	      const rules = path.join(cwd, t.rules);
   111	      const rulesText = readIfFile(rules);
   112	      if (rulesText === null || !rulesText.includes('apriori/runbook.md')) {
   113	        checks.push({ id: 'D4', status: 'finding', detail: `${t.name}: ${t.rules} ${rulesText !== null ? 'lost its runbook pointer' : (fs.existsSync(rules) ? 'is not a regular readable file' : 'is missing')}`, fix: 'apriori init' });
   114	        broken++;
   115	      }
   116	      if (t.command && !isFile(path.join(cwd, t.command))) {
   117	        checks.push({ id: 'D4', status: 'finding', detail: `${t.name}: command file ${t.command} is missing`, fix: 'apriori init' });
   118	        broken++;
   119	      }
   120	    }
   121	    if (!broken) checks.push({ id: 'D4', status: 'ok', detail: `pointers intact for: ${detected.join(', ')}` });
   122	  }
   123	
   124	  // D6 is COMPUTED before the D5 probe (the id-pattern must be validated — and its store
   125	  // scan survived — before any test command runs; display order stays D5 then D6): an
   126	  // uncompilable or terminated config pattern skips the probe entirely.
   127	  const specsDir = path.join(ap, 'specs');
   128	  const d6Entries = [];
   129	  let idPatternBroken = false;
   130	  const idp = resolveIdPattern(cwd, null);
   131	  if (idp.error) {
   132	    idPatternBroken = true;
   133	    d6Entries.push({ id: 'D6', status: 'finding', detail: idp.error, fix: 'fix the id-pattern row in apriori/process-config.md' });
   134	  } else if (!fs.existsSync(specsDir)) d6Entries.push({ id: 'D6', status: 'n/a', detail: 'no store dir — see D2' });
   135	  else {
   136	    const collected = collectScenarios([specsDir], new RegExp(idp.source), makeIdMatcher(idp));
   137	    if (collected.failure) {
   138	      idPatternBroken = true;
   139	      d6Entries.push({ id: 'D6', status: 'finding', detail: matcherFailureMsg(collected.failure), fix: 'fix the id-pattern row in apriori/process-config.md' });
   140	    } else {
   141	      const { byId, unidentified, files, duplicates } = collected;
   142	      const src = ` (id-pattern: ${idp.origin})`;
   143	      if (!files.length || (byId.size === 0 && unidentified.length === 0))
   144	        d6Entries.push({ id: 'D6', status: 'n/a', detail: 'empty store — normal for a new project' });
   145	      else {
   146	        let bad = 0;
   147	        if (unidentified.length) { d6Entries.push({ id: 'D6', status: 'finding', detail: `scenario(s) without a bindable ${boundedSource(idp.source)} ID${src}: ${unidentified.map(([, t]) => t.slice(0, 40)).join(' · ')}`, fix: 'add leading IDs' }); bad++; }
   148	        if (duplicates.length) { d6Entries.push({ id: 'D6', status: 'finding', detail: `duplicate scenario IDs: ${duplicates.map((d) => d.id).join(', ')}`, fix: 'deduplicate IDs' }); bad++; }
   149	        if (!bad) d6Entries.push({ id: 'D6', status: 'ok', detail: `${byId.size} scenario(s), all bindable, no duplicates${src}` });
   150	      }
   151	    }
   152	  }
   153	
   154	  // D5 — TAP probe (the one declared side effect; --no-run and a broken id-pattern skip)
   155	  const cfgCmd = opts.testCmd ? null : configTestCmd(cwd);
   156	  const testCmd = opts.testCmd || (cfgCmd && cfgCmd.error ? null : cfgCmd);
   157	  if (idPatternBroken) checks.push({ id: 'D5', status: 'n/a', detail: 'probe skipped (invalid id-pattern config)' });
   158	  else if (cfgCmd && cfgCmd.error) checks.push({ id: 'D5', status: 'finding', detail: `config: ${cfgCmd.error}`, fix: 'keep one live test-cmd row' });
   159	  // --no-run is judged BEFORE the missing command: an explicit human skip is never a finding,
   160	  // and the missing-command branch below is one.
   161	  else if (opts.noRun) checks.push({ id: 'D5', status: 'n/a', detail: 'probe skipped (--no-run)' });
   162	  // A missing test command is not a neutral absence: it takes `apriori gate` with it. Reporting
   163	  // n/a here is what let a project run a whole change with a gate that never once ran.
   164	  else if (!testCmd) checks.push({ id: 'D5', status: 'finding',
   165	    detail: 'no test command configured — `apriori gate` cannot run C1 (the binding check) at all',
   166	    fix: 'add a test-cmd row to apriori/process-config.md (or pass --test-cmd)' });
   167	  else checks.push(classifyProbe(runTestCommand(testCmd, cwd)));
   168	
   169	  // D6 — store health (computed above, displayed here)
   170	  checks.push(...d6Entries);
   171	
   172	  // D7 — changes overview
   173	  const actives = activeChanges(cwd);
   174	  const notes = [];
   175	  let d7bad = 0;
   176	  const changesRoot = path.join(cwd, 'apriori', 'changes');
   177	  for (const c of actives) {
   178	    const fp = path.join(changesRoot, c, 'flow-state.md');
   179	    if (!fs.existsSync(fp)) { checks.push({ id: 'D7', status: 'finding', detail: `${c}: no flow-state.md`, fix: 'apriori new / restore the file' }); d7bad++; continue; }
   180	    // per-file containment: a symlinked flow-state pointing outside the changes tree is
   181	    // diagnosed, never read (doctor must not leak outside content into its report)
   182	    if (!containsReal(changesRoot, fp)) { checks.push({ id: 'D7', status: 'finding', detail: `${c}: flow-state.md escapes apriori/changes/ (symlink?) — not read`, fix: 'fix the file' }); d7bad++; continue; }
   183	    let st;
   184	    try { st = parseFlowState(fs.readFileSync(fp, 'utf8')); }
   185	    catch (e) { checks.push({ id: 'D7', status: 'finding', detail: `${c}: flow-state unreadable (${e.message})`, fix: 'fix the file' }); d7bad++; continue; }
   186	    if (!st.change) { checks.push({ id: 'D7', status: 'finding', detail: `${c}: flow-state has no 'change:' key`, fix: 'fix the file' }); d7bad++; }
   187	    else if (st.change !== c) { checks.push({ id: 'D7', status: 'finding', detail: `${c}: flow-state says 'change: ${st.change}' — mismatches the dir`, fix: 'fix the file' }); d7bad++; }
   188	    else notes.push(`${c} @ ${st['current-step'] || '?'}`);
   189	  }
   190	  const archRoot = path.join(cwd, 'apriori', 'changes', 'archive');
   191	  if (fs.existsSync(archRoot)) {
   192	    for (const b of fs.readdirSync(archRoot).sort()) {
   193	      if (!/^\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}T\d{4}-/.test(b)) continue;
   194	      const dir = path.join(archRoot, b);
   195	      let isDir = false;
   196	      try { isDir = fs.statSync(dir).isDirectory(); } catch { continue; }
   197	      if (!isDir) continue;
   198	      if (!containsReal(archRoot, dir)) { notes.push(`${b}: skipped (escapes archive/ — symlink?)`); continue; }
   199	      const fp = path.join(dir, 'flow-state.md');
   200	      if (!fs.existsSync(fp)) continue;
   201	      if (!containsReal(archRoot, fp)) { notes.push(`${b}: flow-state escapes archive/ (symlink?) — not read`); continue; }
   202	      const st = parseFlowState(fs.readFileSync(fp, 'utf8'));
   203	      const step = st['current-step'];
   204	      if (step && step !== 'DONE' && step !== 'ABANDONED') notes.push(`archived ${b} @ ${step} — gate ④ possibly pending`);
   205	    }
   206	  }
   207	  if (!d7bad) checks.push(actives.length || notes.length
   208	    ? { id: 'D7', status: 'ok', detail: notes.join('; ') || `${actives.length} active change(s), flow-states valid` }
   209	    : { id: 'D7', status: 'n/a', detail: 'no changes yet' });
   210	  else if (notes.length) checks.push({ id: 'D7', status: 'ok', detail: notes.join('; ') });
   211	
   212	  // D8 — legacy 3.x layout roots (existence-level, never followed or read through)
   213	  const legacy = legacyRoots(cwd);
   214	  checks.push(legacy.length
   215	    ? { id: 'D8', status: 'finding', detail: `legacy 3.x layout root(s) present: ${legacy.join(', ')}`, fix: 'migrate the artifacts into their change bundles — see MIGRATING.md (4.0): https://github.com/Apriorhythm/apriori-spec-development/blob/main/MIGRATING.md' }
   216	    : { id: 'D8', status: 'ok', detail: 'no legacy 3.x layout roots' });
   217	
   218	  return finish();
   219	}
   220	
   221	function toJson(res) {
   222	  return { result: res.result, findings: res.findings, checks: res.checks, errors: res.errors };
   223	}
   224	
   225	const USAGE = 'usage: apriori doctor [--test-cmd "<cmd>"] [--no-run] [--cwd <dir>] [--json]';
   226	
   227	function cli(argv) {
   228	  return withStrict(argv, { sub: 'doctor', usage: USAGE, positionals: 0,
   229	    flags: { '--test-cmd': 'value', '--no-run': 'flag', '--cwd': 'value', '--json': 'flag' },
   230	    jsonError: (m) => JSON.stringify({ result: 'UNUSABLE', findings: 0, checks: [], errors: [m] }, null, 2) }, (f) => {
   231	    const a = { testCmd: f['--test-cmd'] || null, noRun: !!f['--no-run'], cwd: f['--cwd'] || process.cwd(), json: !!f['--json'] };
   232	    const res = runDoctor(a);
   233	    if (a.json) { console.log(JSON.stringify(toJson(res), null, 2)); return res.code; }
   234	    for (const e of res.errors) console.error('doctor: ' + e);
   235	    const mark = { ok: '✓', finding: '✗', 'n/a': '–' };
   236	    for (const c of res.checks) console.log(`${mark[c.status]} ${c.id} ${c.detail}${c.fix ? `  → ${c.fix}` : ''}`);
   237	    console.log(res.code === 2 ? '\nDOCTOR: UNUSABLE (see errors)'
   238	      : res.findings ? `\nDOCTOR: ${res.findings} finding(s)` : '\nDOCTOR: HEALTHY');
   239	    return res.code;
   240	  });
   241	}
   242	
   243	module.exports = { runDoctor, classifyProbe, cli };
     1	'use strict';
     2	/*
     3	 * apriori check — structural consistency checks (JS port of v2's check_docs.py, adapted for V3).
     4	 * Zero deps — pure Node stdlib. Each check returns an array of failure strings.
     5	 *
     6	 * Ported from v2 (behaviour-equivalent): anchor resolution, file-link existence,
     7	 * EN/CN heading alignment, verdict-phrase table + drift variants, codex-command EN/CN parity.
     8	 * V3 changes: CK-04 every spec scenario carries a bindable ID; CK-05 no OpenSpec adapter
     9	 * references remain (the v2 "adapter wording" assertions invert to "no openspec/opsx at all").
    10	 */
    11	const fs = require('fs');
    12	const path = require('path');
    13	const { containsReal } = require('./archive-merge');
    14	const { withStrict } = require('./args');
    15	const { DEFAULT_ID, resolveIdPattern } = require('./config');
    16	const { leadId, makeIdMatcher, matcherFailureMsg } = require('./spec-runner');   // one recognition contract for all consumers
    17	
    18	// ---- helpers (ported from gh_slug / parse / num_prefix) ---------------------
    19	function ghSlug(heading) {
    20	  const s = heading.trim().toLowerCase().replace(/`/g, '').replace(/\*/g, '');
    21	  let out = '';
    22	  for (const ch of s) {
    23	    if (ch === ' ') out += '-';
    24	    else if (ch === '-' || ch === '_') out += ch;
    25	    else if (/[\p{L}\p{N}\p{M}]/u.test(ch)) out += ch;
    26	  }
    27	  return out;
    28	}
    29	function stripFences(text) { return text.replace(/```[\s\S]*?```/g, ''); }
    30	function headings(text) {
    31	  const s = stripFences(text);
    32	  return [...s.matchAll(/^(#{1,6})\s+(.+)$/gm)].map((m) => [m[1].length, m[2]]);
    33	}
    34	function anchors(text) { return [...stripFences(text).matchAll(/\]\(#([^)]+)\)/g)].map((m) => m[1]); }
    35	function fileLinks(text) { return [...stripFences(text).matchAll(/\]\((\.\/[^)#]+)(?:#[^)]*)?\)/g)].map((m) => m[1]); }
    36	function numPrefix(t) { const m = t.trim().match(/^(\d+(?:\.\d+)*)\b/); return m ? m[1] : null; }
    37	function lineOf(text, idx) { return text.slice(0, idx).split('\n').length; }
    38	
    39	// ---- CK-01/CK-09: anchors resolve, file links exist — resolved from the LINKING file's dir;
    40	// cross-file fragments (./y.md#frag) validated against the target's headings (self-mode)
    41	function fileLinksWithFrags(text) {
    42	  return [...stripFences(text).matchAll(/\]\((\.\/[^)#]+)(?:#([^)]*))?\)/g)].map((m) => [m[1], m[2] || null]);
    43	}
    44	function checkLinks(baseDir, name, text) {
    45	  const fails = [];
    46	  const slugs = new Set(headings(text).map(([, t]) => ghSlug(t)));
    47	  for (const a of anchors(text)) if (!slugs.has(a)) fails.push(`${name}: broken anchor #${a}`);
    48	  for (const [f, frag] of fileLinksWithFrags(text)) {
    49	    const target = path.join(baseDir, f);
    50	    if (!fs.existsSync(target)) { fails.push(`${name}: broken file link ${f}`); continue; }
    51	    if (frag) {
    52	      const tslugs = new Set(headings(fs.readFileSync(target, 'utf8')).map(([, t]) => ghSlug(t)));
    53	      if (!tslugs.has(frag)) fails.push(`${name}: broken cross-file fragment ${f}#${frag}`);
    54	    }
    55	  }
    56	  return fails;
    57	}
    58	
    59	// ---- CK-02: EN/CN heading alignment (length, levels, numeric prefixes) -------
    60	function checkHeadingAlignment(enName, enText, cnName, cnText) {
    61	  const fails = [];
    62	  const eh = headings(enText), ch = headings(cnText);
    63	  if (eh.length !== ch.length) fails.push(`heading count mismatch: ${enName}=${eh.length} vs ${cnName}=${ch.length}`);
    64	  const n = Math.min(eh.length, ch.length);
    65	  for (let i = 0; i < n; i++) {
    66	    const [el, et] = eh[i], [cl, ct] = ch[i];
    67	    const ep = numPrefix(et), cp = numPrefix(ct);
    68	    const numOk = (ep && cp) ? ep === cp : true;
    69	    if (el !== cl || !numOk) fails.push(`heading misaligned @#${i + 1}: [h${el}] ${et.slice(0, 40)} <-> [h${cl}] ${ct.slice(0, 40)}`);
    70	  }
    71	  return fails;
    72	}
    73	
    74	// ---- CK-03: verdict phrase table + drift variants + codex-command parity -----
    75	const VERDICT_PHRASES = [
    76	  'VERDICT: no major issues, ready to proceed to execution',
    77	  'VERDICT: no major issues',
    78	  'VERDICT: no spec-vs-code gaps',
    79	  'VERDICT: no findings',
    80	  'VERDICT: gaps found',
    81	  'VERDICT: extraction accepted',
    82	  'VERDICT: extraction rejected',
    83	  'VERDICT: <N> issues open',
    84	];
    85	const FORBIDDEN_VARIANTS = [
    86	  [/ready to execute/g, 'EN drift variant "ready to execute"'],
    87	  [/可进入执行(?!阶段)/g, 'CN drift variant 「可进入执行」 missing 「阶段」'],
    88	];
    89	function checkVerdictPhrases(files) {
    90	  // files: {name -> text}; runbooks must contain every canonical phrase; no drift variants; every VERDICT: string is a table entry
    91	  const fails = [];
    92	  for (const rb of ['RUNBOOK.md', 'RUNBOOK_cn.md'])
    93	    if (files[rb]) for (const p of VERDICT_PHRASES) if (!files[rb].includes(p)) fails.push(`${rb}: phrase-table entry missing: ${p}`);
    94	  for (const [name, text] of Object.entries(files)) {
    95	    for (const [re, label] of FORBIDDEN_VARIANTS)
    96	      for (const m of text.matchAll(re)) fails.push(`${name}:${lineOf(text, m.index)}: ${label}`);
    97	    for (const m of text.matchAll(/VERDICT:[^"'`」\n]+/g)) {
    98	      const q = m[0].replace(/[ .。);,，]+$/, '');
    99	      if (!VERDICT_PHRASES.some((p) => q === p || q.startsWith(p)))
   100	        fails.push(`${name}:${lineOf(text, m.index)}: VERDICT string not in table: ${q}`);
   101	    }
   102	  }
   103	  return fails;
   104	}
   105	
   106	// codex command extraction (ported from codex_candidates)
   107	const CODEX_BARE = new Set(['codex', 'exec', 'resume']);
   108	function codexCandidates(text) {
   109	  const cmds = [];
   110	  for (const line of text.split('\n')) {
   111	    let start = 0, idx;
   112	    while ((idx = line.indexOf('codex exec', start)) >= 0) {
   113	      let frag = line.slice(idx); start = idx + 10;
   114	      frag = frag.replace(/\\"/g, '"').replace(/——/g, ' — ');
   115	      const raw = frag;
   116	      frag = frag.replace(/(^|\s)"[^"]*"/g, '$1').replace(/(^|\s)'[^']*'/g, '$1').replace(/\s#.*$/, '');
   117	      const tokens = [];
   118	      for (let tok of frag.split(/\s+/).filter(Boolean)) {
   119	        tok = tok.replace(/[,;)。]+$/, '');
   120	        const prev = tokens[tokens.length - 1];
   121	        const isFlagVal = prev && prev.startsWith('-') && /^[A-Za-z0-9._/-]+$/.test(tok);
   122	        if (CODEX_BARE.has(tok) || isFlagVal ||
   123	            /^(-{1,2}[A-Za-z][\w-]*|[A-Za-z_]+=("[^"]*"|\S+)|<[^>]+>|\||2>&1|<|\/dev\/null)$/.test(tok)) tokens.push(tok);
   124	        else break;
   125	      }
   126	      const checkable = !raw.includes('...') && !raw.includes('…') && !tokens.includes('--last') &&
   127	        (tokens.some((t) => t.startsWith('-')) || raw.includes('"'));
   128	      if (checkable && tokens.length > 2) cmds.push(tokens);
   129	    }
   130	  }
   131	  return cmds;
   132	}
   133	function checkCodexCommands(enText, cnText) {
   134	  const fails = [];
   135	  const en = codexCandidates(enText), cn = codexCandidates(cnText);
   136	  if (en.length !== cn.length) { fails.push(`codex command count mismatch: ${en.length} vs ${cn.length}`); return fails; }
   137	  for (let i = 0; i < en.length; i++)
   138	    if (en[i].join(' ') !== cn[i].join(' ')) fails.push(`codex command #${i + 1} token mismatch: EN[${en[i]}] CN[${cn[i]}]`);
   139	  return fails;
   140	}
   141	
   142	// ---- CK-03 (checker 8): codex known-good forms (ported from v2) --------------
   143	const CODEX_EXEMPT = [['codex', 'exec', 'resume', '-s', 'read-only', '<session-id>']];
   144	function checkCodexKnownForms(files) {
   145	  const fails = [];
   146	  for (const [name, text] of Object.entries(files)) {
   147	    for (const tokens of codexCandidates(text)) {
   148	      if (!tokens.includes('resume')) continue;
   149	      if (CODEX_EXEMPT.some((e) => e.join(' ') === tokens.join(' '))) continue;
   150	      if (tokens.includes('-s')) fails.push(`${name}: resume command uses -s (rejected on codex >=0.14x): ${tokens.join(' ')}`);
   151	      if (!(tokens.includes('-c') && tokens.includes('sandbox_mode="read-only"')))
   152	        fails.push(`${name}: resume command missing -c sandbox_mode="read-only": ${tokens.join(' ')}`);
   153	    }
   154	  }
   155	  for (const rb of ['RUNBOOK.md', 'RUNBOOK_cn.md'])
   156	    if (files[rb] && !files[rb].includes('< /dev/null')) fails.push(`${rb}: missing the \`< /dev/null\` non-interactive guidance`);
   157	  return fails;
   158	}
   159	
   160	// ---- CK-04: every spec scenario carries a bindable ID -----------------------
   161	// Recognition goes through leadId — the same contract verify/gate/doctor use — never a
   162	// private `^(…)\b` anchoring that could disagree with them at the edges (CK-14).
   163	function checkScenarioIds(specText, name, idPattern = DEFAULT_ID) {
   164	  const idRe = new RegExp(idPattern);
   165	  const fails = [];
   166	  // fenced scenarios are documentation, not spec — same rule as the spec-runner (SR-13)
   167	  for (const m of stripFences(specText).matchAll(/^####\s+Scenario:\s+(.*)$/gm))
   168	    if (leadId(m[1].trim(), idRe) === null) fails.push(`${name}: scenario without a bindable ID: ${m[1].slice(0, 50)}`);
   169	  return fails;
   170	}
   171	
   172	// scenario titles of one spec text, in order (fence rule as above) — the CLI batches
   173	// these through the shared matcher so config-origin patterns stay terminable (CK-16)
   174	function scenarioTitles(specText) {
   175	  return [...stripFences(specText).matchAll(/^####\s+Scenario:\s+(.*)$/gm)].map((m) => m[1]);
   176	}
   177	
   178	// ---- CK-05: no OpenSpec adapter references remain (V3 is single plain-files) --
   179	const OPENSPEC_MARKERS = [/\/opsx:/, /openspec\//, /\(adapter:/i, /OpenSpec adapter/];
   180	function checkNoOpenspec(name, text) {
   181	  const fails = [];
   182	  for (const line of text.split('\n')) for (const re of OPENSPEC_MARKERS)
   183	    if (re.test(line)) { fails.push(`${name}: residual OpenSpec reference: ${line.trim().slice(0, 60)}`); break; }
   184	  return fails;
   185	}
   186	
   187	// ---- CK-10: review-evidence secret tripwire (consumer mode) -------------------
   188	// Three literal formats ONLY — each with an airtight no-legit-match argument. This is a
   189	// tripwire, not a DLP product: fuzzy patterns false-positive on code discussion, which
   190	// trains people to ignore the tripwire. Per-line SUBSTRING scan (secrets embed in output).
   191	const SECRET_PATTERNS = [
   192	  [/AKIA[0-9A-Z]{16}/, 'AWS access key'],
   193	  [/gh[pousr]_[A-Za-z0-9]{36,}/, 'GitHub token'],
   194	  [/-----BEGIN [A-Z ]*PRIVATE KEY-----/, 'private key (PEM)'],
   195	];
   196	function checkReviewSecrets(root) {
   197	  const fails = [], warns = [];
   198	  // bundle layout: evidence lives in every change's review/ dir, active and archived.
   199	  // Root discovery is guarded — escaping/symlinked dirs are warn-skipped like files.
   200	  const roots = [];
   201	  for (const base of [path.join(root, 'apriori', 'changes'), path.join(root, 'apriori', 'changes', 'archive')]) {
   202	    if (!fs.existsSync(base)) continue;
   203	    for (const e of fs.readdirSync(base, { withFileTypes: true })) {
   204	      if (e.name === 'archive') continue;
   205	      const cd = path.join(base, e.name);
   206	      let cst;
   207	      try { cst = fs.lstatSync(cd); } catch { continue; }   // lstat: never follow a symlinked change dir
   208	      if (cst.isSymbolicLink()) {
   209	        warns.push(`review evidence: ${path.relative(root, cd)} is a symlink or escapes the tree — skipped by the secret scan`);
   210	        continue;
   211	      }
   212	      if (!cst.isDirectory()) continue;
   213	      const rd = path.join(cd, 'review');
   214	      let rst;
   215	      try { rst = fs.lstatSync(rd); } catch { continue; }   // lstat: a dangling review symlink warns, plain absence skips
   216	      if (rst.isSymbolicLink() || !containsReal(base, rd)) {
   217	        warns.push(`review evidence: ${path.relative(root, rd)} is a symlink or escapes the tree — skipped by the secret scan`);
   218	        continue;
   219	      }
   220	      roots.push(rd);
   221	    }
   222	  }
   223	  if (!roots.length) return { fails, warns };                  // no bundles → skip
   224	  const walk = (d) => {
   225	    for (const e of fs.readdirSync(d, { withFileTypes: true })) {
   226	      const p = path.join(d, e.name);
   227	      if (e.isSymbolicLink()) { warns.push(`review evidence: ${path.relative(root, p)} is a symlink — skipped by the secret scan`); continue; }
   228	      if (e.isDirectory()) { walk(p); continue; }
   229	      if (!e.isFile()) continue;
   230	      const lines = fs.readFileSync(p, 'utf8').split('\n');
   231	      lines.forEach((ln, i) => {
   232	        for (const [re, label] of SECRET_PATTERNS)
   233	          if (re.test(ln)) fails.push(`${path.relative(root, p)}:${i + 1}: ${label} detected in review evidence — sanitize the raw (see SECURITY.md; rewrite history if already pushed)`);
   234	      });
   235	    }
   236	  };
   237	  for (const rd of roots) walk(rd);
   238	  return { fails, warns };
   239	}
   240	
     1	'use strict';
     2	/*
     3	 * spec-runner — bind spec scenario IDs to test runs, report per-scenario red/green.
     4	 * VISION endgame, weak form: "spec IS the test suite". Zero deps — pure Node stdlib.
     5	 * Coupling surface is tiny and language-agnostic: markdown scenarios + a TAP-emitting test command.
     6	 *
     7	 * FAIL-CLOSED: a deterministic gate must refuse to say GREEN when its inputs are missing,
     8	 * its instrument misbehaved, or its evidence is ambiguous. Vacuous success is a bug.
     9	 * Exit codes: 0 = GREEN · 1 = spec/test gaps · 2 = invocation or infrastructure error.
    10	 */
    11	const fs = require('fs');
    12	const path = require('path');
    13	const { spawnSync } = require('child_process');
    14	// merge-family helpers shared with archive (3.1 projection); archive-merge never requires spec-runner
    15	const am = require('./archive-merge');
    16	const { withStrict } = require('./args');
    17	// DEFAULT_ID lives in config (the zero-dep resolution home) and is re-exported here unchanged
    18	const { DEFAULT_ID, resolveIdPattern, sanitizeMsg } = require('./config');
    19	const SCENARIO_RE = /^####\s+Scenario:\s+(.*)$/gm;
    20	// TAP result line, directive split off separately (# SKIP / # TODO)
    21	const TAP_RE = /^(ok|not ok)\s+\d+\s+-\s+(.*)$/gm;
    22	
    23	// Leading scenario/test ID. The match must end at a word boundary: `XX-01b` is NOT `XX-01` —
    24	// silently truncating would merge two different scenarios into one binding.
    25	function leadId(text, idRe) {
    26	  const m = text.trim().match(idRe);
    27	  if (!m || m.index !== 0) return null;
    28	  const next = text.trim()[m[0].length];
    29	  if (next !== undefined && /[A-Za-z0-9_]/.test(next)) return null;
    30	  return m[0];
    31	}
    32	
    33	function stripFences(text) { return text.replace(/```[\s\S]*?```/g, ''); }
    34	
    35	// ---- the id matcher: one recognition contract, two execution channels -------------------
    36	// A config-sourced pattern is repository input that CI consumes automatically; its every
    37	// actual application runs inside a terminable child (SIGKILL on budget) so a catastrophic
    38	// pattern can never hang the CLI. Flag/default origins are operator/built-in trust: inline.
    39	const CHILD_PATH = path.join(__dirname, 'id-match-child.js');
    40	// the child's spawn contract, exported so tests assert the budget DETERMINISTICALLY
    41	// (a wall-clock window cannot prove the 2000ms figure)
    42	const CHILD_SPAWN_OPTS = Object.freeze({ timeout: 2000, killSignal: 'SIGKILL', shell: false });
    43	let childRunnerOverride = null;                 // test seam — SR-55 injects failures here
    44	function _setChildRunner(fn) { childRunnerOverride = fn; }
    45	function defaultChildRunner(payload) {
    46	  const r = spawnSync(process.execPath, [CHILD_PATH], {
    47	    input: payload, ...CHILD_SPAWN_OPTS,
    48	    encoding: 'utf8', maxBuffer: 8 * 1024 * 1024,
    49	  });
    50	  return { status: r.status, signal: r.signal || null, stdout: r.stdout || '', error: r.error || null };
    51	}
    52	// resolved = resolveIdPattern's success shape → { batch(texts) → {ids:(string|null)[]} | {failure} }
    53	function makeIdMatcher(resolved) {
    54	  if (resolved.origin !== 'config') {
    55	    const re = new RegExp(resolved.source);
    56	    return { batch: (texts) => ({ ids: texts.map((t) => leadId(t, re)) }) };
    57	  }
    58	  return { batch: (texts) => {
    59	    const runner = childRunnerOverride || defaultChildRunner;
    60	    let r;
    61	    try { r = runner(JSON.stringify({ pattern: resolved.source, texts })); }
    62	    catch (e) { return { failure: `spawn-error: ${String(e.message || e)}` }; }
    63	    // timeout FIRST: a real spawnSync timeout carries ETIMEDOUT and/or the kill signal —
    64	    // classifying by r.error first would misreport the termination as a spawn error
    65	    if ((r.error && r.error.code === 'ETIMEDOUT') || r.signal === 'SIGKILL')
    66	      return { failure: 'timeout (budget exceeded, child terminated)' };
    67	    if (r.error) return { failure: `spawn-error: ${String(r.error.message || r.error)}` };
    68	    if (r.signal) return { failure: `signal ${r.signal}` };
    69	    if (r.status !== 0) return { failure: `non-zero-exit (${r.status})` };
    70	    let parsed;
    71	    try { parsed = JSON.parse(r.stdout); } catch { return { failure: 'malformed-output (not JSON)' }; }
    72	    // strict success shape: exactly one JSON document whose ONLY key is a well-formed ids array
    73	    if (!parsed || typeof parsed !== 'object' || Array.isArray(parsed) ||
    74	        Object.keys(parsed).length !== 1 || !Array.isArray(parsed.ids) ||
    75	        parsed.ids.length !== texts.length ||
    76	        parsed.ids.some((x) => x !== null && typeof x !== 'string'))
    77	      return { failure: 'malformed-output (bad shape)' };
    78	    return { ids: parsed.ids };
    79	  } };
    80	}
    81	// the one sanitized failure text every consumer surfaces for a config-origin matcher failure
    82	function matcherFailureMsg(failure) {
    83	  return sanitizeMsg(`process-config id-pattern matching failed (${failure})`);
    84	}
    85	const inlineMatcher = (idRe) => makeIdMatcher({ source: idRe.source, origin: 'default' });
    86	
    87	function mdFiles(target) {
    88	  if (!fs.existsSync(target)) return [];
    89	  const st = fs.statSync(target);
    90	  if (st.isFile()) return [target];
    91	  const out = [];
    92	  for (const e of fs.readdirSync(target, { withFileTypes: true })) {
    93	    const p = path.join(target, e.name);
    94	    if (e.isDirectory()) out.push(...mdFiles(p));
    95	    else if (e.name.endsWith('.md')) out.push(p);
    96	  }
    97	  return out.sort();
    98	}
    99	
   100	// Shared scenario scan over one text — STRUCTURE ONLY (no ID extraction; that is the
   101	// matcher's job so config-origin patterns stay inside the terminable channel).
   102	// Fenced content is documentation; scenarios inside DEPRECATED requirement blocks
   103	// (the marker REMOVED injects) are excluded in every form — they are neither demanded
   104	// nor listed, and their lingering tests become ORPHAN.
   105	function scanTitles(text, label, pairs, preClean) {
   106	  const clean = preClean ? text : am.stripDeprecatedBlocks(stripFences(text));
   107	  let m;
   108	  SCENARIO_RE.lastIndex = 0;
   109	  while ((m = SCENARIO_RE.exec(clean)) !== null) pairs.push([label, m[1]]);
   110	}
   111	
   112	// One batch over tagged pairs ([label,title,scoped]) plus sibling titles (appended to the
   113	// SAME batch — never a third matcher call) → { acc, ids, siblingIds } or { failure }.
   114	function makeThenBind(pairs, matcher, siblingTitles = [], integrityTitles = []) {
   115	  const titles = [...pairs.map((p) => p[1]), ...siblingTitles, ...integrityTitles];
   116	  const res = matcher.batch(titles);
   117	  if (res.failure) return { failure: res.failure };
   118	  const acc = { byId: new Map(), idFiles: new Map(), unidentified: [] };
   119	  pairs.forEach(([label, title], i) => {
   120	    const id = res.ids[i];
   121	    if (id) {
   122	      if (!acc.byId.has(id)) { acc.byId.set(id, []); acc.idFiles.set(id, []); }
   123	      acc.byId.get(id).push(title);
   124	      acc.idFiles.get(id).push(label);
   125	    } else acc.unidentified.push([label, title]);
   126	  });
   127	  const sibSlice = res.ids.slice(pairs.length, pairs.length + siblingTitles.length);
   128	  const siblingIds = new Set(sibSlice.filter((x) => x !== null));
   129	  const integrityIds = res.ids.slice(pairs.length + siblingTitles.length);
   130	  return { acc, ids: res.ids.slice(0, pairs.length), siblingIds, integrityIds };
   131	}
   132	
   133	// Scenario titles a SIBLING active change genuinely declares — an EXEMPTION source, so it
   134	// is strict: each sibling delta must parse cleanly (parseDeltaStrict, zero problems, >0 ops)
   135	// and only scenarios inside its ADDED/MODIFIED block bodies count (a rename-only sibling's
   136	// scenarios already live in this change's projection and bind as out-of-scope reds).
   137	// Anomalies — malformed deltas, symlinks, escaping paths, unreadable files — grant NOTHING:
   138	// skipping is the fail-closed direction here (fewer exemptions = a stricter verdict); they
   139	// are never an ERROR, or a broken sibling could block every other change's gate.
   140	// fault-tolerant .md walk for the sibling scan ONLY: every readdir/lstat failure at any
   141	// depth yields zero entries from that branch — an anomaly must never throw out of verify()
   142	// nor create an infra ERROR (it merely grants no exemption)
   143	function safeMdWalk(dir) {
   144	  const out = [];
   145	  let entries;
   146	  try { entries = fs.readdirSync(dir, { withFileTypes: true }); } catch { return out; }
   147	  for (const e of entries) {
   148	    const p = path.join(dir, e.name);
   149	    try {
   150	      if (e.isDirectory()) out.push(...safeMdWalk(p));
   151	      else if (e.name.endsWith('.md')) out.push(p);
   152	    } catch { /* skip the branch */ }
   153	  }
   154	  return out.sort();
   155	}
   156	
   157	function collectSiblingTitles(cwd, change) {
   158	  const changesDir = path.resolve(cwd, 'apriori', 'changes');
   159	  const titles = [];
   160	  let entries = [];
   161	  try { entries = fs.readdirSync(changesDir, { withFileTypes: true }); } catch { return titles; }
   162	  for (const e of entries) {
   163	    if (!e.isDirectory() || e.name === change || e.name === 'archive') continue;
   164	    const siblingDir = path.join(changesDir, e.name);
   165	    if (!am.containsReal(changesDir, siblingDir)) continue;
   166	    const specsDir = path.join(siblingDir, 'specs');
   167	    let sd; try { sd = fs.lstatSync(specsDir); } catch { continue; }
   168	    if (sd.isSymbolicLink() || !sd.isDirectory() || !am.containsReal(siblingDir, specsDir)) continue;
   169	    for (const f of safeMdWalk(specsDir)) {
   170	      let st; try { st = fs.lstatSync(f); } catch { continue; }
   171	      if (st.isSymbolicLink() || !st.isFile() || !am.containsReal(specsDir, f)) continue;
   172	      let raw; try { raw = fs.readFileSync(f, 'utf8'); } catch { continue; }
   173	      const { delta, problems } = am.parseDeltaStrict(raw);
   174	      if (problems.length || am.deltaOpCount(delta) === 0) continue;   // malformed sibling grants nothing
   175	      const removed = new Set(delta.REMOVED.keys());
   176	      for (const bucket of [delta.ADDED, delta.MODIFIED])
   177	        for (const [name, block] of bucket) {
   178	          if (removed.has(name)) continue;
   179	          scanTitles(block, e.name, titles);
   180	        }
   181	    }
   182	  }
   183	  return titles.map(([, t]) => t);
   184	}
   185	
   186	// Batch-bind (label,title) pairs through a matcher → acc, or {failure} when the matcher fails.
   187	function bindPairs(pairs, matcher) {
   188	  const res = matcher.batch(pairs.map(([, title]) => title));
   189	  if (res.failure) return { failure: res.failure };
   190	  const acc = { byId: new Map(), idFiles: new Map(), unidentified: [] };
   191	  pairs.forEach(([label, title], i) => {
   192	    const id = res.ids[i];
   193	    if (id) {
   194	      if (!acc.byId.has(id)) { acc.byId.set(id, []); acc.idFiles.set(id, []); }
   195	      acc.byId.get(id).push(title);
   196	      acc.idFiles.get(id).push(label);
   197	    } else acc.unidentified.push([label, title]);
   198	  });
   199	  return acc;
   200	}
   201	
   202	function dupsOf(acc) {
   203	  return [...acc.byId.entries()].filter(([, titles]) => titles.length > 1)
   204	    .map(([id]) => ({ id, files: [...new Set(acc.idFiles.get(id))] }));
   205	}
   206	
   207	// Collect scenarios from spec files.
   208	// → { byId, unidentified:[[file,title]], files, missingTargets:[], duplicates:[{id,files}] }
   209	// (+ failure when the matcher's channel fails — callers fail closed on it)
   210	function collectScenarios(specTargets, idRe, matcher) {
   211	  const m = matcher || inlineMatcher(idRe);
   212	  const missingTargets = specTargets.filter((t) => !fs.existsSync(t));
   213	  const files = [...new Set(specTargets.flatMap(mdFiles))].sort();
   214	  const pairs = [];
   215	  for (const f of files) scanTitles(fs.readFileSync(f, 'utf8'), f, pairs);
   216	  const acc = bindPairs(pairs, m);
   217	  if (acc.failure) return { failure: acc.failure, byId: new Map(), unidentified: [], files, missingTargets, duplicates: [] };
   218	  return { byId: acc.byId, unidentified: acc.unidentified, files, missingTargets, duplicates: dupsOf(acc) };
   219	}
   220	
   221	// Same collection over in-memory texts (the projection path) — label = store-relative suffix.
   222	function collectScenariosFromTexts(texts, idRe, matcher) {
   223	  const m = matcher || inlineMatcher(idRe);
   224	  const pairs = [];
   225	  for (const [label, text] of texts) scanTitles(text, label, pairs);
   226	  const acc = bindPairs(pairs, m);
   227	  if (acc.failure) return { failure: acc.failure, byId: new Map(), unidentified: [], files: [...texts.keys()], missingTargets: [], duplicates: [] };
   228	  return { byId: acc.byId, unidentified: acc.unidentified, files: [...texts.keys()], missingTargets: [], duplicates: dupsOf(acc) };
   229	}
   230	
   231	// ---- TAP lexer (tap-contract): TAP is a version-aware protocol, not a pile of regexes ----
   232	const PLAN_LINE_RE = /^1\.\.(\d+)\s*(?:#.*)?$/;
   233	const POINT_LINE_RE = /^(?:ok|not ok)(?:\s|$)/;
   234	const POINT_NUM_RE = /^(?:ok|not ok)\s+(\d+)(?:\s|$)/;   // complete numeric token only — `ok 1abc` stays unnumbered
   235	const SUPPORTED_TAP_VERSIONS = new Set([12, 13, 14]);
   236	
   237	// split at the first UNESCAPED '#' (backslash escapes the next char); decode \\ and \# in the
   238	// description ONLY (other backslash pairs stay literal) — so \# is never a directive delimiter
   239	function splitDirective(rest) {
   240	  let desc = '', directive = null;
   241	  for (let i = 0; i < rest.length; i++) {
   242	    const c = rest[i];
   243	    if (c === '\\' && i + 1 < rest.length) { desc += c + rest[i + 1]; i++; continue; }
   244	    if (c === '#') { directive = rest.slice(i + 1); break; }
   245	    desc += c;
   246	  }
   247	  const isSkipTodo = directive !== null && /^\s*(skip|todo)/i.test(directive);
   248	  const decoded = desc.replace(/\\([\\#])/g, '$1').trim();
   249	  return { desc: decoded, isSkipTodo };
   250	}
   251	
   252	// does a text carry TAP-shaped tokens? (misrouted-channel detection — bail-out included)
   253	function looksLikeTap(text) {
   254	  return /^(?:TAP version |(?:not )?ok\b|1\.\.\d)/m.test(text || '') || /^\s*bail out!/im.test(text || '');
   255	}
   256	
   257	// Lex normalized stdout into typed top-level tokens. Line endings normalize first
   258	// (CRLF and lone-CR both end a line — a failure never hides behind a line-ending style).
   259	function lexTap(stdout) {
   260	  const lines = stdout.replace(/\r\n/g, '\n').replace(/\r/g, '\n').split('\n');
   261	  const tokens = [], problems = [];
   262	  let yamlOpen = -1;
   263	  for (let idx = 0; idx < lines.length; idx++) {
   264	    const line = lines[idx], n = idx + 1;
   265	    if (yamlOpen >= 0) {                                   // inside a top-level YAML block: opaque
   266	      if (line === '...') yamlOpen = -1;
   267	      continue;
   268	    }
   269	    if (/^\s*bail out!/i.test(line) && line.trim()) {      // case-insensitive, indentation-blind
   270	      tokens.push({ kind: 'bailout', line: line.trim(), n }); continue;
   271	    }
   272	    if (/^\s/.test(line)) continue;                        // indented = nested subtest detail
   273	    if (line === '---') { yamlOpen = n; continue; }
   274	    if (line.startsWith('TAP version ')) {
   275	      const m = /^TAP version (\d+)$/.exec(line);
   276	      tokens.push({ kind: 'version', num: m ? Number(m[1]) : null, raw: line, n }); continue;
   277	    }
   278	    let m;
   279	    if ((m = PLAN_LINE_RE.exec(line))) { tokens.push({ kind: 'plan', total: Number(m[1]), n }); continue; }
   280	    if (/^pragma [+-]\w+$/.test(line)) continue;           // the ONLY ignored TAP-14 construct
   281	    if (POINT_LINE_RE.test(line)) {
   282	      const fail = line.startsWith('not ok');
   283	      const nm = POINT_NUM_RE.exec(line);
   284	      let rest = line.slice(fail ? 'not ok'.length : 'ok'.length);
   285	      if (nm) rest = rest.replace(/^\s+\d+/, '');
   286	      rest = rest.replace(/^\s+/, '');
   287	      const { desc, isSkipTodo } = splitDirective(rest);
   288	      const description = desc.replace(/^-\s+/, '');       // the dash separator is optional (TAP)
   289	      tokens.push({ kind: 'point', fail, num: nm ? Number(nm[1]) : null, description, isSkipTodo, line, n });
   290	      continue;
   291	    }
   292	    // anything else at column 0 is diagnostics (comments, prose) — including `ok:`-style prefixes
   293	  }
   294	  if (yamlOpen >= 0) problems.push(`unterminated YAML diagnostic block opened at line ${yamlOpen} — a stray --- must not swallow the rest of the stream`);
   295	  return { tokens, problems };
   296	}
   297	
   298	// Parse TAP stdout → { results, untagged, untaggedFails, unattributedFailures, bailout,
   299	//                      plans, points, dupNumbers, tapProblems }
   300	// (+ matchFailure when the matcher's channel fails — callers fail closed on it)
     1	'use strict';
     2	/*
     3	 * archive-merge — plain-files native implementation of the archive action (§4).
     4	 * Zero deps — pure Node stdlib. Consumes the same delta format the OpenSpec adapter used
     5	 * (## ADDED/MODIFIED/REMOVED/RENAMED Requirements), so it is the adapter-free path.
     6	 *
     7	 * 3.1: also home of the merge-family pure helpers shared with `verify --change` —
     8	 * delta discovery, projection building, CAS base stamps, realpath containment.
     9	 */
    10	const fs = require('fs');
    11	const path = require('path');
    12	const crypto = require('crypto');
    13	const { withStrict } = require('./args');
    14	const { configCas, configCasProblem } = require('./resolve');
    15	
    16	const REQ_RE = /^###\s+Requirement:\s+(.+?)\s*$([\s\S]*?)(?=^###\s+Requirement:|$(?![\s\S]))/gm;
    17	const SECTION_SPLIT_RE = /^##\s+(ADDED|MODIFIED|REMOVED|RENAMED)\s+Requirements\s*$/m;
    18	// the exact marker merge() injects on REMOVED; heading LINE of a deprecated block
    19	const DEPRECATED_RE = /^###\s+Requirement:.*_deprecated \(superseded by [^)]*\)_/;
    20	// a whole deprecated block (heading line + body up to the next requirement)
    21	const DEPRECATED_BLOCK_RE = /^###[ \t]+Requirement:[^\n]*_deprecated \(superseded by [^)]*\)_[^\n]*$[\s\S]*?(?=^###[ \t]+Requirement:|$(?![\s\S]))/gm;
    22	
    23	function stripDeprecatedBlocks(text) { return text.replace(DEPRECATED_BLOCK_RE, ''); }
    24	
    25	// name -> full block (heading + body), in document order; duplicates reported (first block wins)
    26	function parseRequirementsStrict(text) {
    27	  const map = new Map();
    28	  const duplicates = [];
    29	  let m;
    30	  REQ_RE.lastIndex = 0;
    31	  while ((m = REQ_RE.exec(text)) !== null) {
    32	    const name = m[1].trim();
    33	    if (map.has(name)) duplicates.push(name);
    34	    else map.set(name, m[0].replace(/\s+$/, '') + '\n');
    35	  }
    36	  return { map, duplicates };
    37	}
    38	
    39	function parseRequirements(text) { return parseRequirementsStrict(text).map; }
    40	
    41	// ---- CAS base stamps -------------------------------------------------------
    42	
    43	// sha256 over content with line endings normalized — a delta authored on Windows
    44	// must fingerprint identically to the same store checked out with LF
    45	function fingerprint(text) {
    46	  const norm = text.replace(/\r\n/g, '\n').replace(/\r/g, '\n');
    47	  return 'sha256:' + crypto.createHash('sha256').update(norm, 'utf8').digest('hex');
    48	}
    49	
    50	const STAMP_LINE_RE = /^<!--\s*apriori-base:\s*(\S+)\s*-->\s*$/gm;
    51	// any standalone comment line ATTEMPTING to be a stamp — a malformed attempt must be an error,
    52	// never silently treated as "unstamped" (that would quietly disable CAS)
    53	const STAMP_ATTEMPT_RE = /^\s*<!--(?=[^>]*apriori-base)[^>]*-->\s*$/gm;
    54	
    55	// → { stamp: 'sha256:<hex>' | 'new' | null, problems: [] }
    56	// Exactly one stamp line, before the first delta section, well-formed digest.
    57	function parseStamp(text) {
    58	  const problems = [];
    59	  const sectionIdx = text.search(SECTION_SPLIT_RE);
    60	  STAMP_ATTEMPT_RE.lastIndex = 0;
    61	  let m;
    62	  while ((m = STAMP_ATTEMPT_RE.exec(text)) !== null) {
    63	    STAMP_LINE_RE.lastIndex = 0;
    64	    if (!STAMP_LINE_RE.test(m[0].trim())) problems.push(`malformed apriori-base stamp line: ${m[0].trim()}`);
    65	  }
    66	  const found = [];
    67	  STAMP_LINE_RE.lastIndex = 0;
    68	  while ((m = STAMP_LINE_RE.exec(text)) !== null) found.push({ value: m[1], index: m.index });
    69	  if (!found.length) return { stamp: null, problems };
    70	  if (found.length > 1) problems.push('carries more than one apriori-base stamp line');
    71	  for (const f of found) {
    72	    if (sectionIdx >= 0 && f.index > sectionIdx) problems.push('apriori-base stamp appears after the first delta section heading');
    73	    if (f.value !== 'new' && !/^sha256:[0-9a-f]{64}$/.test(f.value)) problems.push(`malformed apriori-base stamp '${f.value}'`);
    74	  }
    75	  return { stamp: problems.length ? null : found[0].value, problems };
    76	}
    77	
    78	// ---- delta parsing ---------------------------------------------------------
    79	
    80	// per-line forms of the shared regexes (no /m — the walker feeds one normalized line at a time)
    81	const SECTION_LINE_RE = /^##\s+(ADDED|MODIFIED|REMOVED|RENAMED)\s+Requirements\s*$/;
    82	const H2_LINE_RE = /^##\s/;
    83	const REQ_LINE_RE = /^###\s+Requirement:\s+(.+?)\s*$/;
    84	const SCENARIO_LINE_RE = /^####\s+Scenario:/;
    85	const RENAME_LINE_RE = /^\s*-\s*(.+?)\s*->\s*(.+?)\s*$/;
    86	const STAMP_ATTEMPT_LINE_RE = /^\s*<!--(?=[^>]*apriori-base)[^>]*-->\s*$/;
    87	const STAMP_STRICT_LINE_RE = /^<!--\s*apriori-base:\s*(\S+)\s*-->\s*$/;
    88	const FENCE_LINE_RE = /^\s*```/;
    89	
    90	// strict form: a sequential, fully-consuming line walker — every line (outside
    91	// code fences, which are opaque) belongs to exactly one legal construct, and
    92	// anything else is a problems[] entry carrying its 1-based line number.
    93	// Well-formed deltas parse byte-identically to the old split()-based grammar.
    94	function parseDeltaStrict(text) {
    95	  const problems = [];
    96	  const buckets = { ADDED: new Map(), MODIFIED: new Map(), REMOVED: new Map(), RENAMED: [] };
    97	  const seen = new Map();               // name -> first section carrying it
    98	  const lines = text.split('\n').map((l) => (l.endsWith('\r') ? l.slice(0, -1) : l));
    99	
   100	  let state = 'FILE_PREAMBLE';          // | 'IN_SECTION' | 'IN_REQUIREMENT' | 'SKIP_UNRECOGNIZED'
   101	  let kind = null;                      // current section kind while IN_SECTION/IN_REQUIREMENT
   102	  let blockName = null, blockLines = null, blockDiscard = false;
   103	  let inFence = false;
   104	  let stamp = null, stampSeen = false, stampProblems = false, sectionSeen = false;
   105	
   106	  const flush = () => {
   107	    if (blockLines === null) return;
   108	    if (!blockDiscard) buckets[kind].set(blockName, blockLines.join('\n').replace(/\s+$/, '') + '\n');
   109	    blockName = null; blockLines = null; blockDiscard = false;
   110	  };
   111	  const stampProblem = (msg) => { problems.push(msg); stampProblems = true; };
   112	
   113	  for (let i = 0; i < lines.length; i++) {
   114	    const line = lines[i], n = i + 1;
   115	    if (inFence) {                      // opaque: never structure, never a problem
   116	      if (blockLines !== null) blockLines.push(line);
   117	      if (FENCE_LINE_RE.test(line)) inFence = false;
   118	      continue;
   119	    }
   120	    if (FENCE_LINE_RE.test(line)) {
   121	      inFence = true;
   122	      if (blockLines !== null) blockLines.push(line);
   123	      continue;
   124	    }
   125	    const sec = SECTION_LINE_RE.exec(line);
   126	    if (sec) { flush(); state = 'IN_SECTION'; kind = sec[1]; sectionSeen = true; continue; }
   127	    if (H2_LINE_RE.test(line)) {        // one problem per unrecognized heading; its lines are skipped, not re-homed
   128	      flush();
   129	      problems.push(`line ${n}: unrecognized section heading '${line.trim()}'`);
   130	      state = 'SKIP_UNRECOGNIZED'; kind = null;
   131	      continue;
   132	    }
   133	    if (state === 'SKIP_UNRECOGNIZED') continue;   // covered by the heading's problem — no flood, no attribution (stamps included)
   134	    // stamps are matched in every remaining state so a stray one is never absorbed as body text
   135	    if (STAMP_ATTEMPT_LINE_RE.test(line)) {
   136	      const m = STAMP_STRICT_LINE_RE.exec(line.trim());
   137	      if (!m) { stampProblem(`line ${n}: malformed apriori-base stamp line: ${line.trim()}`); continue; }
   138	      if (stampSeen) { stampProblem(`line ${n}: carries more than one apriori-base stamp line`); continue; }
   139	      stampSeen = true;
   140	      if (sectionSeen) { stampProblem(`line ${n}: apriori-base stamp appears after the first delta section heading`); continue; }
   141	      if (m[1] !== 'new' && !/^sha256:[0-9a-f]{64}$/.test(m[1])) { stampProblem(`line ${n}: malformed apriori-base stamp '${m[1]}'`); continue; }
   142	      stamp = m[1];
   143	      continue;
   144	    }
   145	    const req = REQ_LINE_RE.exec(line);
   146	    if (req) {
   147	      if (state === 'FILE_PREAMBLE') { problems.push(`line ${n}: requirement block before any section heading`); continue; }
   148	      if (kind === 'RENAMED') {
   149	        // the whole illegal block is discarded — its body must not be reinterpreted as rename lines
   150	        problems.push(`line ${n}: requirement block inside the RENAMED section`);
   151	        flush();
   152	        state = 'IN_REQUIREMENT'; blockName = null; blockLines = []; blockDiscard = true;
   153	        continue;
   154	      }
   155	      flush();
   156	      state = 'IN_REQUIREMENT';
   157	      blockName = req[1].trim(); blockLines = [line]; blockDiscard = false;
   158	      if (buckets[kind].has(blockName)) {          // first block wins, as before
   159	        problems.push(`line ${n}: duplicate requirement '${blockName}' in the ${kind} section`);
   160	        blockDiscard = true;
   161	      } else if (seen.has(blockName)) {
   162	        problems.push(`line ${n}: duplicate requirement '${blockName}' appears in both ${seen.get(blockName)} and ${kind}`);
   163	      } else seen.set(blockName, kind);
   164	      continue;
   165	    }
   166	    if (state === 'IN_REQUIREMENT') { blockLines.push(line); continue; }   // scenarios and prose are body
   167	    if (SCENARIO_LINE_RE.test(line)) { problems.push(`line ${n}: scenario outside any requirement block`); continue; }
   168	    if (state === 'IN_SECTION' && kind === 'RENAMED') {
   169	      const rn = RENAME_LINE_RE.exec(line);
   170	      if (rn) buckets.RENAMED.push([rn[1].trim(), rn[2].trim()]);
   171	      continue;                          // other lines are free text
   172	    }
   173	    // FILE_PREAMBLE / section preamble free text and blanks are legal
   174	  }
   175	  flush();
   176	  return { delta: buckets, stamp: stampProblems ? null : stamp, problems };
   177	}
   178	
   179	function parseDelta(text) { return parseDeltaStrict(text).delta; }
   180	
   181	// total number of operations a parsed delta carries (0 = nothing recognized/parseable)
   182	function deltaOpCount(delta) {
   183	  return delta.ADDED.size + delta.MODIFIED.size + delta.REMOVED.size + delta.RENAMED.length;
   184	}
   185	
   186	// mutation ops can silently clobber concurrent edits; ADDED-only deltas conflict or no-op instead
   187	function mutationOpCount(delta) {
   188	  return delta.MODIFIED.size + delta.REMOVED.size + delta.RENAMED.length;
   189	}
   190	
   191	// the one warning text every surface shares (WARN grade this minor; 4.0 makes stamps mandatory)
   192	// 4.0: unstamped mutation deltas are DENIED by default; --no-cas or the config row waives visibly
   193	// waiver source, or {problem} when the config itself is broken (deny + name it) — the
   194	// explicit flag stays supreme even over a broken config (the flag is a human's will)
   195	function casWaiver(cwd, noCas) {
   196	  if (noCas) return { waiver: '--no-cas' };
   197	  const problem = configCasProblem(cwd);
   198	  if (problem) return { problem };
   199	  if (configCas(cwd) === 'optional') return { waiver: 'process-config cas: optional' };
   200	  return {};
   201	}
   202	
   203	function unstampedWarning(suffix) {
   204	  return `unstamped mutation delta ${suffix} — divergence undetectable; run: apriori stamp <store-file>`;
   205	}
   206	
   207	// ---- merge (pure) ----------------------------------------------------------
   208	
   209	// Pure merge. Returns { store: Map, merged, modified, deprecated, renamed, conflicts, unchanged }
   210	function merge(storeText, delta, change) {
   211	  const parsed = parseRequirementsStrict(storeText);
   212	  const store = parsed.map;
   213	  const merged = [], modified = [], deprecated = [], renamed = [], conflicts = [], unchanged = [];
   214	  // a corrupt store (same requirement name twice) must never be silently collapsed
   215	  for (const d of parsed.duplicates) conflicts.push(`duplicate requirement '${d}' in the store (corrupt store)`);
   216	  const renamedTargets = new Set();   // names created by THIS run's renames — an ADDED hitting one is a same-delta collision, never a rerun no-op
   217	  // RENAMED first: rename the store block's ID, preserving its content
   218	  for (const [oldName, newName] of (delta.RENAMED || [])) {
   219	    if (!store.has(oldName)) {
   220	      // rerun signature: source already gone AND target present → this rename already happened
   221	      if (store.has(newName)) { unchanged.push(`${oldName} -> ${newName} (already renamed)`); continue; }
   222	      conflicts.push(`RENAMED '${oldName}' has no target in store`); continue;
   223	    }
   224	    if (store.has(newName)) { conflicts.push(`RENAMED target '${newName}' already exists in store`); continue; }
   225	    const block = store.get(oldName);
   226	    const renamedBlock = block.replace(/^###\s+Requirement:\s+.+$/m, `### Requirement: ${newName}`);
   227	    // rebuild the map preserving order, swapping the key at the old position
   228	    const rebuilt = new Map();
   229	    for (const [k, v] of store) rebuilt.set(k === oldName ? newName : k, k === oldName ? renamedBlock : v);
   230	    store.clear();
   231	    for (const [k, v] of rebuilt) store.set(k, v);
   232	    renamed.push(`${oldName} -> ${newName}`);
   233	    renamedTargets.add(newName);
   234	  }
   235	  for (const [name, block] of delta.ADDED) {
   236	    if (store.has(name)) {
   237	      // idempotent rerun: identical content means this delta already merged — a no-op, not a collision.
   238	      // But a name just created by THIS run's RENAMED is a same-delta collision, never a rerun.
   239	      if (!renamedTargets.has(name) && store.get(name).trim() === block.trim()) unchanged.push(name);
   240	      else conflicts.push(`ADDED '${name}' already exists in store`);
   241	    } else { store.set(name, block); merged.push(name); }
   242	  }
   243	  for (const [name, block] of delta.MODIFIED) {
   244	    if (!store.has(name)) conflicts.push(`MODIFIED '${name}' has no target in store`);
   245	    // already applied (rerun signature) — same trim comparison the ADDED no-op uses
   246	    else if (store.get(name).trim() === block.trim()) unchanged.push(name);
   247	    else { store.set(name, block); modified.push(name); }
   248	  }
   249	  for (const name of delta.REMOVED.keys()) {
   250	    if (!store.has(name)) {
   251	      // rerun signature: already deprecated BY THIS CHANGE → no-op; by another change → real collision
   252	      const depByThis = `${name}  _deprecated (superseded by ${change})_`;
   253	      if (store.has(depByThis)) { unchanged.push(`${name} (already deprecated)`); continue; }
   254	      const depOther = [...store.keys()].find((k) => k.startsWith(`${name}  _deprecated (superseded by `));
   255	      if (depOther) { conflicts.push(`REMOVED '${name}' was already deprecated by a different change ('${depOther}')`); continue; }
   256	      conflicts.push(`REMOVED '${name}' has no target in store`);
   257	    } else {
   258	      const block = store.get(name);
   259	      const nl = block.indexOf('\n');
   260	      store.set(name, block.slice(0, nl) + `  _deprecated (superseded by ${change})_` + block.slice(nl));
   261	      deprecated.push(name);
   262	    }
   263	  }
   264	  return { store, merged, modified, deprecated, renamed, conflicts, unchanged };
   265	}
   266	
   267	function renderStore(storeText, store) {
   268	  const idx = storeText.indexOf('### Requirement:');
   269	  const header = idx >= 0 ? storeText.slice(0, idx) : '';
   270	  return header + [...store.values()].join('\n');
   271	}
   272	
   273	// ---- paths and discovery ---------------------------------------------------
   274	
   275	// Realpath containment: target (followed through symlinks; not-yet-existing targets judged
   276	// by their nearest existing ancestor) must resolve STRICTLY inside root. Fail-closed.
   277	function containsReal(root, target) {
   278	  let realRoot;
   279	  try { realRoot = fs.realpathSync(root); } catch { return false; }
   280	  let cur = path.resolve(target);
   281	  const rest = [];
   282	  while (!fs.existsSync(cur)) {
   283	    const parent = path.dirname(cur);
   284	    if (parent === cur) return false;
   285	    rest.unshift(path.basename(cur));
   286	    cur = parent;
   287	  }
   288	  let realCur;
   289	  try { realCur = fs.realpathSync(cur); } catch { return false; }
   290	  const full = rest.length ? path.join(realCur, ...rest) : realCur;
   291	  return full !== realRoot && (full + path.sep).startsWith(realRoot + path.sep);
   292	}
   293

   295	  const out = [];
   296	  const walk = (d) => {
   297	    for (const e of fs.readdirSync(d, { withFileTypes: true })) {
   298	      const p = path.join(d, e.name);
   299	      // Dirents reflect lstat: a symlink is neither file nor directory. Follow it explicitly —
   300	      // silently skipping a symlinked dir would hide deltas (fail-open); containment judges the target.

FILE:apriori/specs/archive-merge/spec.md
### Requirement: archive-merge applies delta specs to the living store
`apriori archive` SHALL merge a change's delta requirements into the living spec store by stable Requirement ID, consuming the same `## ADDED/MODIFIED/REMOVED/RENAMED Requirements` delta format the OpenSpec adapter used, so it is the adapter-free native implementation of §4's archive action.

#### Scenario: AM-01 ADDED appends a new requirement
- WHEN the delta has an ADDED requirement whose ID is absent from the store
- THEN it is appended and the ID is listed as merged

#### Scenario: AM-02 MODIFIED replaces the existing block
- WHEN the delta has a MODIFIED requirement whose ID exists in the store
- THEN the whole store block is replaced and the ID is listed as modified

#### Scenario: AM-03 REMOVED marks the block deprecated, not deleted
- WHEN the delta has a REMOVED requirement present in the store
- THEN the store block is kept and marked `deprecated (superseded by <change>)`, listed as deprecated

#### Scenario: AM-04 same-ID conflict stops without writing
- WHEN an ADDED ID already exists, or a MODIFIED/REMOVED target is missing
- THEN the merge reports the conflict, writes nothing, and exits 1 (a human opens a ledger issue)

#### Scenario: AM-05 the action lists every merged/modified/deprecated ID
- WHEN a merge succeeds
- THEN every affected Requirement ID is printed by category (§4 requires the action list them)

#### Scenario: AM-06 archive moves the in-flight change under a dated archive dir
- WHEN archive completes on change `<name>`
- THEN the in-flight `apriori/changes/<name>/` (bare name, no prefix) is moved to `apriori/changes/archive/<YYYY-MM-DDThhmm>-<name>/` — a colon-free date-time stamped by the CLI's own clock (never an agent guess); e.g. `archive/2026-07-06T0657-add-playback/`

#### Scenario: AM-07 RENAMED renames a requirement in place, preserving content
- WHEN the delta has a `## RENAMED Requirements` entry `- Old Name -> New Name` and `Old Name` exists while `New Name` does not
- THEN the store block keeps its content but its heading ID becomes `New Name`, listed as renamed; a missing source (with no existing target) or a colliding target is a conflict (stop, exit 1, nothing written) — the source-gone-AND-target-present rerun case is AM-10's no-op, not a conflict

#### Scenario: AM-08 a content-bearing delta that parses to zero operations is a hard error
- WHEN `apriori archive` reads a delta file that has non-whitespace content but matches no recognized section (e.g. wrong heading level or keyword, so 0 delta operations parse)
- THEN it writes nothing, prints an error naming the expected `## ADDED|MODIFIED|REMOVED|RENAMED Requirements` + `### Requirement:` format, and exits non-zero — never reporting MERGED for a delta the parser did not understand

#### Scenario: AM-09 the first archive in a repo creates the store file
- WHEN `apriori archive --write` targets a store file that does not exist yet
- THEN it notes the store will be created, starts from an empty store, and writes the merged result — no ENOENT

#### Scenario: AM-10 re-running an already-merged delta is an idempotent no-op
- WHEN an ADDED requirement in the delta already exists in the store with byte-identical (trimmed) content, or a RENAMED entry's source is gone while its target is present, or a REMOVED entry's target is gone while its deprecated form marked `superseded by <THIS change>` is present — each the signature of a rerun after a partial archive
- THEN each is reported as already merged / already renamed / already deprecated (no-op), not a conflict, and the run can proceed to move the change dir; but an ADDED whose name was created by THIS run's own RENAMED is a same-delta collision and still conflicts even with identical content, and a REMOVED target deprecated by a DIFFERENT change remains a conflict

#### Scenario: AM-11 change names are validated and the dir move cannot escape
- WHEN `--change` is not bare kebab-case (e.g. `../victim`), or `--changes-dir` is given but the change dir does not exist
- THEN archive exits 2 before writing anything; the move helper independently rejects any source that resolves outside the changes dir

#### Scenario: AM-12 the store commit and the dir move are one transaction (single-file form)
- WHEN the single-file form `apriori archive --store <f> --delta <f> --change <name> --write --changes-dir <dir>` runs and the change-dir move fails
- THEN the store on disk stays byte-for-byte untouched (staged to a temp file, committed by rename only after the move succeeds) and no temp residue remains — the high-level `--change` form's move-failure rule is AM-18's (stores already committed stay committed)

### Requirement: high-level archive merges a whole change transactionally
`apriori archive --change <name>` SHALL discover every `.md` delta file under `<changes-dir>/<name>/specs/` (`--changes-dir` defaults to `apriori/changes` and sets both the discovery root and the move root), map each to its store target `apriori/specs/<suffix>` by path suffix, dry-run the whole set by default, and on `--write` commit in four phases — preflight (validate, parse, CAS-check, merge in memory; any failure anywhere → nothing written), stage (write every `<store>.tmp-archive`), commit (rename each temp in sorted path order), move (only when `--changes-dir` was explicitly passed, only after all stores committed). The guarantee is failure-atomicity up to the commit point; crash durability is not claimed.

#### Scenario: AM-13 dry-run reports the whole change and writes nothing
- WHEN `apriori archive --change <name>` runs without `--write` on a change spanning several modules
- THEN it prints per-module merged/modified/deprecated/renamed/no-op requirement names and a result line, and no file on disk changes

#### Scenario: AM-14 any preflight failure means nothing is written
- WHEN any module's merge reports a conflict, any delta file fails a hygiene guard, or any CAS stamp mismatches during `--write`
- THEN every failure across all modules is reported, no store file changes, and the exit code is 1

#### Scenario: AM-15 a mid-commit failure is reported exactly
- WHEN a rename in the commit phase fails after earlier renames succeeded
- THEN archive reports exactly which modules committed, which did not, and which temp files remain for manual completion, and exits 1 — already-committed renames are not rolled back

#### Scenario: AM-16 a pre-existing temp file blocks the run
- WHEN any target `<store>.tmp-archive` already exists at preflight
- THEN archive exits 1 naming that file, writes nothing, and leaves the pre-existing file untouched (it may be another run in flight or a manual-recovery artifact)

#### Scenario: AM-17 zero discovered delta files fail closed
- WHEN `<changes-dir>/<name>/specs/` exists but contains no `.md` delta files, or the change dir itself is missing
- THEN archive exits 2 naming the searched path

#### Scenario: AM-18 the change-dir move waits for every store commit
- WHEN `--write --changes-dir <dir>` succeeds
- THEN the change dir moves to `<dir>/archive/<stamp>-<name>/` only after ALL stores committed; without an explicit `--changes-dir` no move happens; a move failure leaves the committed stores in place and exits 1

#### Scenario: AM-19 high-level and single-file forms are mutually exclusive
- WHEN `--change` is combined with `--store` or `--delta`
- THEN archive exits 2 with usage; the single-file form by itself keeps its 3.0.1 behavior unchanged

#### Scenario: AM-20 per-file delta hygiene guards the whole set
- WHEN any discovered delta file is empty/whitespace-only, parses to zero operations despite content, or carries duplicate requirement names (within a section or across sections)
- THEN the run refuses naming the file (and requirement, for duplicates), nothing is written, and no partial projection or merge survives

#### Scenario: AM-21 duplicate requirement names in the store are corruption
- WHEN the store text handed to any merge contains the same requirement name twice
- THEN the merge reports it as a conflict instead of silently keeping the last block

#### Scenario: AM-22 realpath containment governs every participating path
- WHEN the change dir, a delta file, a mapped store target, or the move destination (`<changes-dir>/archive/…` — e.g. an `archive/` that is a symlink pointing outside) — each followed through symlinks — resolves outside its root (changes dir / `apriori/specs/`), or a not-yet-existing path's nearest existing ancestor resolves outside its root
- THEN the command exits 2 naming the offending path, and nothing is read as spec input, written, or moved

### Requirement: CAS base stamps detect store divergence
A delta file MAY carry exactly one base stamp `<!-- apriori-base: sha256:<64 lowercase hex> -->` or `<!-- apriori-base: new -->` before its first `## <OP> Requirements` section, declaring the fingerprint of the store file it was authored against. The fingerprint SHALL be SHA-256 over the store file's content with line endings normalized (`\r\n` and lone `\r` → `\n`). `apriori verify --change`, `apriori archive --change`, and single-file `apriori archive` SHALL check present stamps; an absent stamp means no check. `apriori stamp <store-file>` SHALL print the stamp line matching the file's current content.

#### Scenario: AM-23 malformed or duplicated stamps are hygiene errors
- WHEN a delta file carries more than one stamp line, a stamp after the first section heading, or a malformed digest
- THEN the file is rejected by the hygiene guard (nothing projected or written) naming the file

#### Scenario: AM-24 a diverged stamp stops archive before any write, on both surfaces
- WHEN a stamped delta's store file fingerprint no longer matches (including a `new` stamp whose store now exists) — whether archive is invoked as `--change <name>` or as single-file `--store <f> --delta <f>`
- THEN each form reports the store path with expected vs actual fingerprint, writes nothing, and exits 1 — the §4.11 serialize rule made mechanical; both surfaces are exercised, not just one

#### Scenario: AM-25 stamp-free ADDED-only deltas keep the pre-3.1 behavior
- WHEN a delta carries no base stamp — in the high-level `--change` form and in the single-file form alike
- THEN no CAS divergence check runs for that file; an ADDED-only delta merges exactly as pre-3.1, while a stamp-free MUTATION delta falls to the deny-by-default rule (its own requirement) — "no stamp, no check" never means "no stamp, no rules"

#### Scenario: AM-26 the new sentinel matches only an absent store
- WHEN a delta is stamped `<!-- apriori-base: new -->`
- THEN the check passes while the mapped store file does not exist and fails as a mismatch once it does

#### Scenario: AM-27 apriori stamp prints the current stamp line
- WHEN `apriori stamp <store-file>` runs on an existing readable file, an absent path, a directory, or with a wrong argument count
- THEN it prints the exact matching stamp line (absent path → the `new` form) and exits 0; a directory or unreadable file → error naming the path, exit 2; zero or multiple arguments → usage, exit 2

### Requirement: the delta parser consumes its whole input
`parseDeltaStrict` SHALL be a sequential, fully-consuming parser: every line (outside code fences, which are opaque) belongs to exactly one legal construct — file preamble (stamp/blank/free text without structure markers), a legal `## ADDED|MODIFIED|REMOVED|RENAMED Requirements` heading, section-preamble free text, a `### Requirement:` block (body runs to the next heading or EOF), or a RENAMED `- Old -> New` line — and anything else is a `problems[]` entry carrying its 1-based line number. The exhaustive problem set: unrecognized h2 headings (one problem per heading; its lines are never absorbed into any bucket), requirement/scenario markers before any section, a requirement block inside RENAMED, a scenario marker outside any requirement, duplicate requirement names, and the stamp problems (which also gain line numbers). Both `verify --change` (exit 2) and `archive` (exit 1) inherit the failure.

#### Scenario: AM-28 a misspelled section heading is reported, never absorbed
- WHEN a delta contains `## ADDDED Requirements` (or any other unrecognized h2) followed by requirement blocks
- THEN parsing reports the heading text with its line number, those requirements appear in NO bucket, and archive/verify fail closed

#### Scenario: AM-29 structure outside its legal home is reported with line numbers
- WHEN a `### Requirement:` appears before any section or inside a RENAMED section, or a `#### Scenario:` appears in a section preamble outside any requirement
- THEN each is a problem carrying its 1-based line number; multiple distinct problems are all reported

#### Scenario: AM-30 free text and fences stay legal; the corpus stays clean
- WHEN a delta carries titles/notes in the file preamble, section preambles, or requirement bodies, or fenced content containing heading-like lines
- THEN no problem is reported — and every archived delta in this repo's `apriori/changes/archive/*/specs/` parses with zero problems (the regression corpus)

#### Scenario: AM-31 stamp problems carry line numbers
- WHEN a delta carries a malformed, duplicated, or misplaced apriori-base stamp
- THEN each stamp problem names its 1-based line number

### Requirement: unstamped mutation deltas are denied by default and reruns of applied stamps repair
A delta carrying MODIFIED/REMOVED/RENAMED operations without a CAS stamp SHALL be **denied by default on every archive surface** — the high-level `archive --change` form AND the single-file `--store --delta` form: preflight-grade error (exit 1) naming the file and the cure (`apriori stamp <store-file>`), with **nothing written, staged, or moved**. A visible waiver downgrades to the former warn-and-merge behavior: the archive CLI's `--no-cas` flag or a `| cas | optional |` row in `apriori/process-config.md` (the flag wins), and the output names which waiver source applied. ADDED-only deltas stay exempt (the exemption is clobber-focused: they conflict or no-op, never silently overwrite). `verify --change` (the read-only projection) keeps warning without judging; `buildProjection` still returns `unstampedMutations: string[]`. `merge()`'s MODIFIED op SHALL report `unchanged` when the delta block trim-equals the current store block. Archive preflight SHALL classify each stamped delta per file: stamp matches → merge as today; stamp mismatches with EVERY op `unchanged` → a note (`rerun accepted`) and the run proceeds, move included; stamp mismatches with ANY real pending op → error, the whole preflight fails with nothing written or moved — divergence with pending work is never repaired, and notes stay distinct from errors in the diagnostics.

#### Scenario: AM-32 unstamped mutation deltas are denied on both archive forms
- WHEN an unstamped delta carrying a MODIFIED op is archived via --change or via --store/--delta with no waiver
- THEN each form errors (exit 1) naming the file and the stamp cure, writes/stages/moves nothing, and an unstamped ADDED-only delta merges without complaint

#### Scenario: AM-40 the waiver is visible and downgrades to warn-and-merge
