- THEN the matcher child ran EXACTLY twice — first the full projection-title batch (once, never re-run for the scoped view), second the TAP-description batch — the sentinel counted exactly one test spawn, exactly one projection was built, and both views agree on the same TAP snapshot; a title-batch failure spawns no test (0 spawn), a TAP-batch failure comes after exactly 1 spawn; the invalid-pattern and projection-failure paths spawn nothing (existing assertions)

#### Scenario: SR-64 the JSON contract is pinned for all four outcome classes
- WHEN `--change --json` runs end GREEN, GAPS, pre-test ERROR, and post-TAP ERROR
- THEN GREEN/GAPS carry top-level change-scoped `clean/result/boundGreen/boundRed/unbound/orphan/unidentified/unattributedFailures/duplicates` plus `storeReport` (six classes; array length = count; `boundGreen` a bare count; `unattributedFailures` keeps `{count, lines}`) and `changeScope` (`requirements` as sorted `{file, name, operations[]}`, `scenarioIds` sorted); BOTH ERROR classes omit `storeReport` and `changeScope` entirely (absent, never null); `projection` keeps its existing shape; a `--specs --json` run is unchanged and never carries either field — proven byte-level: golden captures of `--specs` human and JSON outputs (GREEN/GAPS/ERROR, duplicates/unidentified/unattributed/stderr classes) taken against state A compare byte-identical after the change, and the `--specs` run object's own properties never include `storeReport`/`changeScope`

### Requirement: verify --change carries the modified-block integrity report
On a trustworthy `--change` run, `verify` SHALL attach the integrity engine's report for every MODIFIED operation (rename-then-modify targets included, keyed by the post-rename name with the pre-rename block as baseline). The old blocks' scenario titles ride the EXISTING single title batch as a fourth tag class that never enters binding accumulation, scenario counts or the change scope — the two-batch matcher contract stands. JSON: `modifiedIntegrity` is ALWAYS present on GREEN/GAPS (one entry per MODIFIED operation, empty-diff entries included; `file` = store-relative suffix; element shapes, value choices and orderings per the engine's contract; missingLines carry full untruncated text) and ABSENT on every ERROR class and on every `--specs` run (byte goldens continue to prove the latter). Human: a `— MODIFIED INTEGRITY —` section prints an entry when any of dropped/missingLines/ambiguous/titleChanged is non-empty (`!` prefix on dropped/missingLines/ambiguous lines; a plain one-line note per titleChanged; counts only for retained/added), stays entirely silent when every entry's four classes are empty, and truncates lines the state-A way (`slice(0,119)+'…'`). The report never changes the verdict or the exit code.

#### Scenario: SR-65 the report rides the run without touching the verdict
- WHEN a `--change` run's MODIFIED replacement drops KV-02 and loses one AND line, while every scoped scenario is bound green (TAP covering the replacement's scenarios, exit 0)
- THEN the run is still GREEN exit 0; `modifiedIntegrity` carries dropped=[KV-02] and the missing line with full text; the human section prints them with `!`; and removing one scoped test flips the same run to GAPS exit 1 with the identical report attached

#### Scenario: SR-66 presence follows the outcome class
- WHEN `--change --json` runs end GREEN, GAPS, pre-test ERROR and post-TAP ERROR, and a `--specs --json` run executes
- THEN GREEN/GAPS carry `modifiedIntegrity` (an ADDED-only delta yields `[]`; an idempotent MODIFIED — the trim-equal path AND the stamped-rerun repaired path alike — yields its empty-diff entry); both ERROR classes and the `--specs` run omit the field entirely (absent, never null)

#### Scenario: SR-67 the old-block titles join the one batch and bind nothing
- WHEN a config-origin `--change` run with a MODIFIED delta executes with a counting child seam
- THEN the matcher child still runs EXACTLY twice, the first payload contains the OLD block's titles alongside the projection and sibling titles, and the old titles appear in no binding class, no scenario count, no change scope and no store report

#### Scenario: SR-68 the frozen live-specimen fixture reports exactly as hand-derived
- WHEN the engine runs over the frozen copy of the verify-change-scope change's real MODIFIED block (the nine-scenario `projected verify` requirement) against its frozen old store block
- THEN the report deep-equals the hand-derived expectation: retained = ALL NINE scenarios (the two rewrites changed only THEN lines, never titles — hand derivation corrected the earlier assumption), titleChanged=[], dropped=[], added=[], ambiguous=[], and missingLines = exactly the two replaced old THEN lines (scenario SR-16's and scenario SR-18's), full text

### Requirement: a scoped verdict judges a named scenario set, nothing else
`lib/spec-runner.js` SHALL expose `scopedEvaluate(byId, results, scope)`: given the store projection, one TAP snapshot and an explicit set of scenario IDs, it classifies ONLY the scope — each scoped ID lands in bound-green, bound-red or unbound by the same rules the whole-store verdict uses (a scenario whose only results are skips is UNBOUND), and the verdict is clean exactly when the scope carries no red and no unbound member. Store-wide classes are NOT scope classes: results outside the scope are never orphans of a scoped run, and store-wide unidentified scenarios never dirty it — the whole-store verdict keeps its own meaning, unchanged. A scope naming an ID absent from the projection is a caller error surfaced as `missing`, never a silent pass. An empty scope is clean by construction and says so.

#### Scenario: SR-69 the scope is judged and the rest of the store is not
- WHEN the store carries five scenarios of which two are red and one unbound, and the scope names one green scenario
- THEN the scoped verdict is clean with that one scenario bound-green — the store's red and unbound members are outside the scope and outside the verdict

#### Scenario: SR-70 red and unbound inside the scope dirty the scoped verdict
- WHEN the scope names a scenario whose TAP result fails, and separately one with no result at all, and separately one whose only results are skips
- THEN each scoped verdict is unclean, classifying the member as bound-red, unbound and unbound respectively

#### Scenario: SR-71 out-of-scope results are not orphans and an empty scope is clean
- WHEN the TAP snapshot carries results for IDs the scope does not name (some absent from the store entirely), and separately the scope is empty
- THEN the first verdict reports no orphans and stays clean; the second is clean by construction with every class empty


codex
The first pass confirms the stated branch/lineage, but it also exposes two high-risk contracts to pin down: the default pattern’s exact-match semantics across all call paths, and the parser’s state transitions outside `IN_REQUIREMENT`. I’m narrowing those now rather than relying on the requirement’s summaries.

RUNBOOK_cn.md:244:- **CAS 基线章(串行规则的工具化):**写增量规格时,先跑 `apriori stamp apriori/specs/<module>/spec.md`,把打印出的 `<!-- apriori-base: … -->` 行贴在增量文件顶部(第一个 `## … Requirements` 小节之前;规格库文件尚不存在时用 `new`)。此后 `verify --change` 和 `archive` 都会在规格库自增量作成以来已发生变化时拒绝执行——§4.11 的串行规则由此机械化。强制规则:不盖章的**变更类**增量(MODIFIED/REMOVED/RENAMED)**默认被拒绝**——`archive` 在预检即拒、什么都不写,gate C7 同样拦截;两个可见豁免是 `--no-cas` flag 与配置行 `| cas | optional |`(flag 优先,输出会点名生效的豁免源)。`verify --change` 保持只告警不裁决。已完全落地的盖章增量可以干净重跑(不匹配降级为 rerun-accepted 提示)。
RUNBOOK.md:254:- **CAS base stamps (serialize-rule tooling):** when authoring a delta, run `apriori stamp apriori/specs/<module>/spec.md` and paste the printed `<!-- apriori-base: … -->` line at the top of the delta file (before the first `## … Requirements` section; `new` for a not-yet-existing store). Both `verify --change` and `archive` then refuse if the store has diverged since the delta was authored — §4.11's serialize rule made mechanical. Enforcement: unstamped MUTATION deltas (MODIFIED/REMOVED/RENAMED) are **denied by default** — `archive` refuses at preflight with nothing written, and gate C7 blocks; the two visible waivers are the `--no-cas` flag and a `| cas | optional |` config row (the flag wins, and the output names which waiver applied). `verify --change` stays informative (warns, never judges). A stamped delta already fully applied re-runs cleanly (the mismatch downgrades to a rerun-accepted note).

apriori/truth/doctor.md:21:**id-pattern (gate-id-pattern, 2026-08-13).** D6 scans the store with the effective id-pattern (config row > DEFAULT_ID; no flag) and its detail names the source (`(id-pattern: config)` / `(id-pattern: default)`); the unidentified finding echoes the actual source bounded. apriori/truth/spec-runner.md:10:- `leadId(text, idRe)` → leading ID or null. Word-boundary strict: `XX-01b`, `XX-01_x` are NOT `XX-01`.
apriori/truth/spec-runner.md:21:- `verify(opts {specs:[], change, testCmd, idPattern, cwd})` — with `change`: builds the projection via archive-merge's discoverDeltas/buildProjection (roots `<cwd>/apriori/{changes,specs}`); ANY projection failure (validation/hygiene/CAS/merge-conflict) → run with `errors` set and NO test executed; success → normal pipeline over projected texts; run gains `projection: {change, modules, conflicts}`. Without `change`: unchanged. → run object `{ verdict, results, fileCount, failCount, scenarioCount, missingTargets, duplicates, bailout, noTap, exec{status,signal,error}, errors }`. `failCount` = tagged fails + `untaggedFails`.
apriori/truth/spec-runner.md:28:- `DEFAULT_ID` = `[A-Z]+-\d+` — DEFINED in `lib/config.js` (the zero-dep resolution home) and re-exported here unchanged. apriori/truth/spec-runner.md:29:- `makeIdMatcher(resolved)` → `{ batch(texts) → {ids:(string|null)[]} | {failure} }` — ONE recognition contract (leadId semantics) on two channels: flag/default origins inline; CONFIG origin runs every actual application inside a terminable child (`lib/id-match-child.js`, spawned `shell:false` per frozen `CHILD_SPAWN_OPTS` {timeout:2000, killSignal:SIGKILL}, stdin `{pattern,texts}` → stdout strictly `{ids}` single-key, `ids.length === texts.length`, elements string|null). Failure classes — timeout (ETIMEDOUT/SIGKILL classified FIRST), spawn-error, signal, non-zero-exit, malformed-output — all fail closed through `matcherFailureMsg` (sanitized, names process-config). `_setChildRunner` is the test seam. Exported: `makeIdMatcher`, `_setChildRunner`, `matcherFailureMsg`, `CHILD_SPAWN_OPTS`.
apriori/truth/spec-runner.md:45:- D-SR-8 (active): the change projection has exactly ONE implementation, and every consumer reaches it through a call-time resolver rather than a captured reference. A second implementation would drift on delta validation, CAS mismatch, conflicts, or a newly added projection field, and the first casualty would be gate's C7 — which consumes `unstampedMutations` and would silently un-block. Identity is proven mechanically (both paths pass one wrapper; the consumer's source is statically checked for the raw primitives), not by convention. Ratified in gate-degrades (2026-08-14).
apriori/truth/archive-merge.md:7:**Responsibility.** Merge a change's delta spec into the living store by stable Requirement ID (STEP6 archive action); move the change dir to `changes/archive/`. Zero deps. 3.1: also the home of the merge-family pure helpers shared with `verify --change` — delta discovery, projection building, CAS base stamps, realpath containment — plus the high-level `archive --change` transaction and the `stamp` subcommand.
apriori/truth/archive-merge.md:9:**Delta format.** `## ADDED|MODIFIED|REMOVED|RENAMED Requirements` (h2 sections) containing `### Requirement: <name>` blocks (h3); RENAMED carries `- Old -> New` lines; optionally ONE CAS stamp line `<!-- apriori-base: sha256:<64hex> | new -->` before the first section. Parsing: `parseDeltaStrict(text)` → `{ delta, stamp, problems[] }` — since delta-consumption (3.3.x) a sequential fully-consuming line walker: every line outside opaque ``` fences belongs to exactly one legal construct (file preamble, legal section heading, section-preamble free text, requirement block, RENAMED rename line), and anything else — unrecognized h2 headings (one problem per heading, its lines attributed to NOTHING, stamps included), requirement/scenario markers outside their legal home, duplicate requirement names (within/across sections), and stamp problems (malformed/duplicate/late, matched in every non-skipped state) — is a `problems[]` entry carrying its 1-based line number; CRLF parses identically to LF; well-formed deltas parse byte-identically to the pre-walker grammar; `parseDelta(text)` = its delta only; `parseRequirementsStrict(text)` → `{ map, duplicates[] }` (first block wins, duplicates reported); `parseRequirements` = its map; `deltaOpCount(delta)` → total parsed ops (0 = no recognized section OR recognized sections with no parseable requirement/rename lines). `parseStamp(text)` → `{ stamp, problems }` — any standalone comment line ATTEMPTING apriori-base that fails the strict form is a problem, never silently 'unstamped'. `fingerprint(text)` → `sha256:<64hex>` over CRLF/CR→LF-normalized content.
apriori/truth/archive-merge.md:25:- `buildProjection(storeRoot, deltaFiles, change)` → `{ texts:Map<suffix,text>, modules, perModule, conflicts, casMismatches, hygiene, validation, unstampedMutations, notes, repaired }` — all store files pass through; each delta strict-parsed, CAS-checked (mismatch+all-ops-unchanged → repaired/noted instead of failed), merged in memory; nothing touches disk.
apriori/truth/archive-merge.md:26:- `archiveChange({cwd, change, changesDir, changesDirExplicit, write, ops})` → `{ code, out[], err[] }` — four phases, NO staging phase and NO post-commit writes of any kind (change-bundle, 4.0.0): the change dir is a complete bundle (requirement/ history, review/ evidence, gap-report.md, specs/ already inside), so the single atomic move carries everything; the command reads and writes nothing under any legacy root, is track-agnostic, and never deletes `spike/` (executor protocol, pre-archive). The four phases: preflight (discovery+hygiene+CAS+merges+pre-existing-temp guard+destination containment; any failure → nothing written), stage (`<store>.tmp-archive`, this-run cleanup on failure), commit (sorted per-file atomic renames; mid-commit failure → exact committed/not-committed/temps report, NO rollback), move (only changesDirExplicit, only after all commits). `ops` ({writeFileSync,renameSync,rmSync}) is the fault-injection seam.
apriori/truth/archive-merge.md:28:- `cli(argv)`: high-level form `--change <name> [--write] [--changes-dir <dir>]` (no --store/--delta) → archiveChange; single-file form `--store <FILE> --delta <FILE> --change <name> [--write] [--changes-dir <dir>]` unchanged from 3.0.1 EXCEPT it now checks a present CAS stamp (mismatch → exit 1, nothing written) and rejects malformed deltas via parseDeltaStrict problems. Mixing --change with only one of --store/--delta → exit 2. Exit: 0 merged/no-op · 1 conflict/CAS/hygiene/zero-op/stage/commit/move-failure/pre-existing-temp · 2 usage/invalid name/missing change dir/containment.
apriori/truth/archive-merge.md:38:- CAS enforcement is GRADED since cas-enforcement (3.3.x): an unstamped delta STILL MERGES, but mutation-carrying ones (MODIFIED/REMOVED/RENAMED — ADDED-only exempt, the exemption is clobber-focused) warn on every surface (`unstampedWarning(suffix)`, both archive forms + verify --change stderr) and `buildProjection` returns `unstampedMutations: string[]` (suffixes) that gate C7 denies by default (--no-cas / `| cas | optional |` waive it, visibly). Stamps become mandatory in 4.0. Rerun repair: a stamped file whose base mismatches but whose EVERY op is `unchanged` (the already-applied signature; MODIFIED now reports `unchanged` on trim-equality) downgrades to a `rerun accepted` note in `notes[]`/`repaired[]` and contributes no writes — the move phase completes; a mismatch with ANY real pending op still fails the whole preflight.

apriori/specs/archive-merge/spec.md:53:`apriori archive --change <name>` SHALL discover every `.md` delta file under `<changes-dir>/<name>/specs/` (`--changes-dir` defaults to `apriori/changes` and sets both the discovery root and the move root), map each to its store target `apriori/specs/<suffix>` by path suffix, dry-run the whole set by default, and on `--write` commit in four phases — preflight (validate, parse, CAS-check, merge in memory; any failure anywhere → nothing written), stage (write every `<store>.tmp-archive`), commit (rename each temp in sorted path order), move (only when `--changes-dir` was explicitly passed, only after all stores committed). apriori/specs/archive-merge/spec.md:95:### Requirement: CAS base stamps detect store divergence
apriori/specs/archive-merge/spec.md:96:A delta file MAY carry exactly one base stamp `<!-- apriori-base: sha256:<64 lowercase hex> -->` or `<!-- apriori-base: new -->` before its first `## <OP> Requirements` section, declaring the fingerprint of the store file it was authored against. The fingerprint SHALL be SHA-256 over the store file's content with line endings normalized (`\r\n` and lone `\r` → `\n`). `apriori verify --change`, `apriori archive --change`, and single-file `apriori archive` SHALL check present stamps; an absent stamp means no check. `apriori stamp <store-file>` SHALL print the stamp line matching the file's current content.
apriori/specs/archive-merge/spec.md:108:- THEN no CAS divergence check runs for that file; an ADDED-only delta merges exactly as pre-3.1, while a stamp-free MUTATION delta falls to the deny-by-default rule (its own requirement) — "no stamp, no check" never means "no stamp, no rules"
apriori/specs/archive-merge/spec.md:111:- WHEN a delta is stamped `<!-- apriori-base: new -->`
apriori/specs/archive-merge/spec.md:119:`parseDeltaStrict` SHALL be a sequential, fully-consuming parser: every line (outside code fences, which are opaque) belongs to exactly one legal construct — file preamble (stamp/blank/free text without structure markers), a legal `## ADDED|MODIFIED|REMOVED|RENAMED Requirements` heading, section-preamble free text, a `### Requirement:` block (body runs to the next heading or EOF), or a RENAMED `- Old -> New` line — and anything else is a `problems[]` entry carrying its 1-based line number. The exhaustive problem set: unrecognized h2 headings (one problem per heading; its lines are never absorbed into any bucket), requirement/scenario markers before any section, a requirement block inside RENAMED, a scenario marker outside any requirement, duplicate requirement names, and the stamp problems (which also gain line numbers). Both `verify --change` (exit 2) and `archive` (exit 1) inherit the failure.
apriori/specs/archive-merge/spec.md:134:- WHEN a delta carries a malformed, duplicated, or misplaced apriori-base stamp
apriori/specs/archive-merge/spec.md:138:A delta carrying MODIFIED/REMOVED/RENAMED operations without a CAS stamp SHALL be **denied by default on every archive surface** — the high-level `archive --change` form AND the single-file `--store --delta` form: preflight-grade error (exit 1) naming the file and the cure (`apriori stamp <store-file>`), with **nothing written, staged, or moved**. A visible waiver downgrades to the former warn-and-merge behavior: the archive CLI's `--no-cas` flag or a `| cas | optional |` row in `apriori/process-config.md` (the flag wins), and the output names which waiver source applied. ADDED-only deltas stay exempt (the exemption is clobber-focused: they conflict or no-op, never silently overwrite). `verify --change` (the read-only projection) keeps warning without judging; `buildProjection` still returns `unstampedMutations: string[]`. `merge()`'s MODIFIED op SHALL report `unchanged` when the delta block trim-equals the current store block. Archive preflight SHALL classify each stamped delta per file: stamp matches → merge as today; stamp mismatches with EVERY op `unchanged` → a note (`rerun accepted`) and the run proceeds, move included; stamp mismatches with ANY real pending op → error, the whole preflight fails with nothing written or moved — divergence with pending work is never repaired, and notes stay distinct from errors in the diagnostics.

    41	// ---- CAS base stamps -------------------------------------------------------
    42	
    43	// sha256 over content with line endings normalized — a delta authored on Windows
    44	// must fingerprint identically to the same store checked out with LF
    45	function fingerprint(text) {
    46	  const norm = text.replace(/\r\n/g, '\n').replace(/\r/g, '\n');
    47	  return 'sha256:' + crypto.createHash('sha256').update(norm, 'utf8').digest('hex');
    48	}

    50	const STAMP_LINE_RE = /^<!--\s*apriori-base:\s*(\S+)\s*-->\s*$/gm;
    51	// any standalone comment line ATTEMPTING to be a stamp — a malformed attempt must be an error,
    52	// never silently treated as "unstamped" (that would quietly disable CAS)
    53	const STAMP_ATTEMPT_RE = /^\s*<!--(?=[^>]*apriori-base)[^>]*-->\s*$/gm;
    54	
    55	// → { stamp: 'sha256:<hex>' | 'new' | null, problems: [] }
    56	// Exactly one stamp line, before the first delta section, well-formed digest.
    57	function parseStamp(text) {
    58	  const problems = [];
    59	  const sectionIdx = text.search(SECTION_SPLIT_RE);
    60	  STAMP_ATTEMPT_RE.lastIndex = 0;
    61	  let m;
    62	  while ((m = STAMP_ATTEMPT_RE.exec(text)) !== null) {
    63	    STAMP_LINE_RE.lastIndex = 0;
    64	    if (!STAMP_LINE_RE.test(m[0].trim())) problems.push(`malformed apriori-base stamp line: ${m[0].trim()}`);
    65	  }
    66	  const found = [];
    67	  STAMP_LINE_RE.lastIndex = 0;
    68	  while ((m = STAMP_LINE_RE.exec(text)) !== null) found.push({ value: m[1], index: m.index });
    69	  if (!found.length) return { stamp: null, problems };
    70	  if (found.length > 1) problems.push('carries more than one apriori-base stamp line');
    71	  for (const f of found) {
    72	    if (sectionIdx >= 0 && f.index > sectionIdx) problems.push('apriori-base stamp appears after the first delta section heading');
    73	    if (f.value !== 'new' && !/^sha256:[0-9a-f]{64}$/.test(f.value)) problems.push(`malformed apriori-base stamp '${f.value}'`);
    74	  }
    75	  return { stamp: problems.length ? null : found[0].value, problems };
    76	}

    78	// ---- delta parsing ---------------------------------------------------------
    79	
    80	// per-line forms of the shared regexes (no /m — the walker feeds one normalized line at a time)
    81	const SECTION_LINE_RE = /^##\s+(ADDED|MODIFIED|REMOVED|RENAMED)\s+Requirements\s*$/;
    82	const H2_LINE_RE = /^##\s/;
    83	const REQ_LINE_RE = /^###\s+Requirement:\s+(.+?)\s*$/;
    84	const SCENARIO_LINE_RE = /^####\s+Scenario:/;
    85	const RENAME_LINE_RE = /^\s*-\s*(.+?)\s*->\s*(.+?)\s*$/;
    86	const STAMP_ATTEMPT_LINE_RE = /^\s*<!--(?=[^>]*apriori-base)[^>]*-->\s*$/;
    87	const STAMP_STRICT_LINE_RE = /^<!--\s*apriori-base:\s*(\S+)\s*-->\s*$/;
    88	const FENCE_LINE_RE = /^\s*```/;
    89	
    90	// strict form: a sequential, fully-consuming line walker — every line (outside
    91	// code fences, which are opaque) belongs to exactly one legal construct, and
    92	// anything else is a problems[] entry carrying its 1-based line number.
    93	// Well-formed deltas parse byte-identically to the old split()-based grammar.
    94	function parseDeltaStrict(text) {
    95	  const problems = [];
    96	  const buckets = { ADDED: new Map(), MODIFIED: new Map(), REMOVED: new Map(), RENAMED: [] };
    97	  const seen = new Map();               // name -> first section carrying it
    98	  const lines = text.split('\n').map((l) => (l.endsWith('\r') ? l.slice(0, -1) : l));
    99	
   100	  let state = 'FILE_PREAMBLE';          // | 'IN_SECTION' | 'IN_REQUIREMENT' | 'SKIP_UNRECOGNIZED'
   101	  let kind = null;                      // current section kind while IN_SECTION/IN_REQUIREMENT
   102	  let blockName = null, blockLines = null, blockDiscard = false;
   103	  let inFence = false;
   104	  let stamp = null, stampSeen = false, stampProblems = false, sectionSeen = false;
   105	
   106	  const flush = () => {
   107	    if (blockLines === null) return;
   108	    if (!blockDiscard) buckets[kind].set(blockName, blockLines.join('\n').replace(/\s+$/, '') + '\n');
   109	    blockName = null; blockLines = null; blockDiscard = false;
   110	  };
   111	  const stampProblem = (msg) => { problems.push(msg); stampProblems = true; };
   112	
   113	  for (let i = 0; i < lines.length; i++) {
   114	    const line = lines[i], n = i + 1;
   115	    if (inFence) {                      // opaque: never structure, never a problem
   116	      if (blockLines !== null) blockLines.push(line);
   117	      if (FENCE_LINE_RE.test(line)) inFence = false;
   118	      continue;
   119	    }
   120	    if (FENCE_LINE_RE.test(line)) {
   121	      inFence = true;
   122	      if (blockLines !== null) blockLines.push(line);
   123	      continue;
   124	    }
   125	    const sec = SECTION_LINE_RE.exec(line);
   126	    if (sec) { flush(); state = 'IN_SECTION'; kind = sec[1]; sectionSeen = true; continue; }
   127	    if (H2_LINE_RE.test(line)) {        // one problem per unrecognized heading; its lines are skipped, not re-homed
   128	      flush();
   129	      problems.push(`line ${n}: unrecognized section heading '${line.trim()}'`);
   130	      state = 'SKIP_UNRECOGNIZED'; kind = null;
   131	      continue;
   132	    }
   133	    if (state === 'SKIP_UNRECOGNIZED') continue;   // covered by the heading's problem — no flood, no attribution (stamps included)
   134	    // stamps are matched in every remaining state so a stray one is never absorbed as body text
   135	    if (STAMP_ATTEMPT_LINE_RE.test(line)) {
   136	      const m = STAMP_STRICT_LINE_RE.exec(line.trim());
   137	      if (!m) { stampProblem(`line ${n}: malformed apriori-base stamp line: ${line.trim()}`); continue; }
   138	      if (stampSeen) { stampProblem(`line ${n}: carries more than one apriori-base stamp line`); continue; }
   139	      stampSeen = true;
   140	      if (sectionSeen) { stampProblem(`line ${n}: apriori-base stamp appears after the first delta section heading`); continue; }
   141	      if (m[1] !== 'new' && !/^sha256:[0-9a-f]{64}$/.test(m[1])) { stampProblem(`line ${n}: malformed apriori-base stamp '${m[1]}'`); continue; }
   142	      stamp = m[1];
   143	      continue;
   144	    }
   145	    const req = REQ_LINE_RE.exec(line);
   146	    if (req) {
   147	      if (state === 'FILE_PREAMBLE') { problems.push(`line ${n}: requirement block before any section heading`); continue; }
   148	      if (kind === 'RENAMED') {
   149	        // the whole illegal block is discarded — its body must not be reinterpreted as rename lines
   150	        problems.push(`line ${n}: requirement block inside the RENAMED section`);
   151	        flush();
   152	        state = 'IN_REQUIREMENT'; blockName = null; blockLines = []; blockDiscard = true;
   153	        continue;
   154	      }
   155	      flush();
   156	      state = 'IN_REQUIREMENT';
   157	      blockName = req[1].trim(); blockLines = [line]; blockDiscard = false;
   158	      if (buckets[kind].has(blockName)) {          // first block wins, as before
   159	        problems.push(`line ${n}: duplicate requirement '${blockName}' in the ${kind} section`);
   160	        blockDiscard = true;
   161	      } else if (seen.has(blockName)) {
   162	        problems.push(`line ${n}: duplicate requirement '${blockName}' appears in both ${seen.get(blockName)} and ${kind}`);
   163	      } else seen.set(blockName, kind);
   164	      continue;
   165	    }
   166	    if (state === 'IN_REQUIREMENT') { blockLines.push(line); continue; }   // scenarios and prose are body
   167	    if (SCENARIO_LINE_RE.test(line)) { problems.push(`line ${n}: scenario outside any requirement block`); continue; }
   168	    if (state === 'IN_SECTION' && kind === 'RENAMED') {
   169	      const rn = RENAME_LINE_RE.exec(line);
   170	      if (rn) buckets.RENAMED.push([rn[1].trim(), rn[2].trim()]);
   171	      continue;                          // other lines are free text
   172	    }
   173	    // FILE_PREAMBLE / section preamble free text and blanks are legal
   174	  }
   175	  flush();
   176	  return { delta: buckets, stamp: stampProblems ? null : stamp, problems };
   177	}
   178	
   179	function parseDelta(text) { return parseDeltaStrict(text).delta; }
   180	
   181	// total number of operations a parsed delta carries (0 = nothing recognized/parseable)
   182	function deltaOpCount(delta) {
   183	  return delta.ADDED.size + delta.MODIFIED.size + delta.REMOVED.size + delta.RENAMED.length;
   184	}
   185	
   186	// mutation ops can silently clobber concurrent edits; ADDED-only deltas conflict or no-op instead
   187	function mutationOpCount(delta) {
   188	  return delta.MODIFIED.size + delta.REMOVED.size + delta.RENAMED.length;
   189	}

     1	'use strict';
     2	/*
     3	 * spec-runner — bind spec scenario IDs to test runs, report per-scenario red/green.
     4	 * VISION endgame, weak form: "spec IS the test suite". Zero deps — pure Node stdlib.
     5	 * Coupling surface is tiny and language-agnostic: markdown scenarios + a TAP-emitting test command.
     6	 *
     7	 * FAIL-CLOSED: a deterministic gate must refuse to say GREEN when its inputs are missing,
     8	 * its instrument misbehaved, or its evidence is ambiguous. Vacuous success is a bug.
     9	 * Exit codes: 0 = GREEN · 1 = spec/test gaps · 2 = invocation or infrastructure error.
    10	 */
    11	const fs = require('fs');
    12	const path = require('path');
    13	const { spawnSync } = require('child_process');
    14	// merge-family helpers shared with archive (3.1 projection); archive-merge never requires spec-runner
    15	const am = require('./archive-merge');
    16	const { withStrict } = require('./args');
    17	// DEFAULT_ID lives in config (the zero-dep resolution home) and is re-exported here unchanged
    18	const { DEFAULT_ID, resolveIdPattern, sanitizeMsg } = require('./config');
    19	const SCENARIO_RE = /^####\s+Scenario:\s+(.*)$/gm;
    20	// TAP result line, directive split off separately (# SKIP / # TODO)
    21	const TAP_RE = /^(ok|not ok)\s+\d+\s+-\s+(.*)$/gm;
    22	
    23	// Leading scenario/test ID. The match must end at a word boundary: `XX-01b` is NOT `XX-01` —
    24	// silently truncating would merge two different scenarios into one binding.
    25	function leadId(text, idRe) {
    26	  const m = text.trim().match(idRe);
    27	  if (!m || m.index !== 0) return null;
    28	  const next = text.trim()[m[0].length];
    29	  if (next !== undefined && /[A-Za-z0-9_]/.test(next)) return null;
    30	  return m[0];
    31	}
    32	
    33	function stripFences(text) { return text.replace(/```[\s\S]*?```/g, ''); }
    34	
    35	// ---- the id matcher: one recognition contract, two execution channels -------------------
    36	// A config-sourced pattern is repository input that CI consumes automatically; its every
    37	// actual application runs inside a terminable child (SIGKILL on budget) so a catastrophic
    38	// pattern can never hang the CLI. Flag/default origins are operator/built-in trust: inline.
    39	const CHILD_PATH = path.join(__dirname, 'id-match-child.js');
    40	// the child's spawn contract, exported so tests assert the budget DETERMINISTICALLY
    41	// (a wall-clock window cannot prove the 2000ms figure)
    42	const CHILD_SPAWN_OPTS = Object.freeze({ timeout: 2000, killSignal: 'SIGKILL', shell: false });
    43	let childRunnerOverride = null;                 // test seam — SR-55 injects failures here
    44	function _setChildRunner(fn) { childRunnerOverride = fn; }
    45	function defaultChildRunner(payload) {
    46	  const r = spawnSync(process.execPath, [CHILD_PATH], {
    47	    input: payload, ...CHILD_SPAWN_OPTS,
    48	    encoding: 'utf8', maxBuffer: 8 * 1024 * 1024,
    49	  });
    50	  return { status: r.status, signal: r.signal || null, stdout: r.stdout || '', error: r.error || null };
    51	}
    52	// resolved = resolveIdPattern's success shape → { batch(texts) → {ids:(string|null)[]} | {failure} }
    53	function makeIdMatcher(resolved) {
    54	  if (resolved.origin !== 'config') {
    55	    const re = new RegExp(resolved.source);
    56	    return { batch: (texts) => ({ ids: texts.map((t) => leadId(t, re)) }) };
    57	  }
    58	  return { batch: (texts) => {
    59	    const runner = childRunnerOverride || defaultChildRunner;
    60	    let r;
    61	    try { r = runner(JSON.stringify({ pattern: resolved.source, texts })); }
    62	    catch (e) { return { failure: `spawn-error: ${String(e.message || e)}` }; }
    63	    // timeout FIRST: a real spawnSync timeout carries ETIMEDOUT and/or the kill signal —
    64	    // classifying by r.error first would misreport the termination as a spawn error
    65	    if ((r.error && r.error.code === 'ETIMEDOUT') || r.signal === 'SIGKILL')
    66	      return { failure: 'timeout (budget exceeded, child terminated)' };
    67	    if (r.error) return { failure: `spawn-error: ${String(r.error.message || r.error)}` };
    68	    if (r.signal) return { failure: `signal ${r.signal}` };
    69	    if (r.status !== 0) return { failure: `non-zero-exit (${r.status})` };
    70	    let parsed;
    71	    try { parsed = JSON.parse(r.stdout); } catch { return { failure: 'malformed-output (not JSON)' }; }
    72	    // strict success shape: exactly one JSON document whose ONLY key is a well-formed ids array
    73	    if (!parsed || typeof parsed !== 'object' || Array.isArray(parsed) ||
    74	        Object.keys(parsed).length !== 1 || !Array.isArray(parsed.ids) ||
    75	        parsed.ids.length !== texts.length ||
    76	        parsed.ids.some((x) => x !== null && typeof x !== 'string'))
    77	      return { failure: 'malformed-output (bad shape)' };
    78	    return { ids: parsed.ids };
    79	  } };
    80	}
    81	// the one sanitized failure text every consumer surfaces for a config-origin matcher failure
    82	function matcherFailureMsg(failure) {
    83	  return sanitizeMsg(`process-config id-pattern matching failed (${failure})`);
    84	}
    85	const inlineMatcher = (idRe) => makeIdMatcher({ source: idRe.source, origin: 'default' });
    86	
    87	function mdFiles(target) {
    88	  if (!fs.existsSync(target)) return [];
    89	  const st = fs.statSync(target);
    90	  if (st.isFile()) return [target];
    91	  const out = [];
    92	  for (const e of fs.readdirSync(target, { withFileTypes: true })) {
    93	    const p = path.join(target, e.name);
    94	    if (e.isDirectory()) out.push(...mdFiles(p));
    95	    else if (e.name.endsWith('.md')) out.push(p);
    96	  }
    97	  return out.sort();
    98	}

   100	// Shared scenario scan over one text — STRUCTURE ONLY (no ID extraction; that is the
   101	// matcher's job so config-origin patterns stay inside the terminable channel).
   102	// Fenced content is documentation; scenarios inside DEPRECATED requirement blocks
   103	// (the marker REMOVED injects) are excluded in every form — they are neither demanded
   104	// nor listed, and their lingering tests become ORPHAN.
   105	function scanTitles(text, label, pairs, preClean) {
   105	function scanTitles(text, label, pairs, preClean) {
   106	  const clean = preClean ? text : am.stripDeprecatedBlocks(stripFences(text));
   107	  let m;
   108	  SCENARIO_RE.lastIndex = 0;
   109	  while ((m = SCENARIO_RE.exec(clean)) !== null) pairs.push([label, m[1]]);
   110	}

   112	// One batch over tagged pairs ([label,title,scoped]) plus sibling titles (appended to the
   113	// SAME batch — never a third matcher call) → { acc, ids, siblingIds } or { failure }.
   114	function makeThenBind(pairs, matcher, siblingTitles = [], integrityTitles = []) {
   115	  const titles = [...pairs.map((p) => p[1]), ...siblingTitles, ...integrityTitles];
   116	  const res = matcher.batch(titles);
   117	  if (res.failure) return { failure: res.failure };
   118	  const acc = { byId: new Map(), idFiles: new Map(), unidentified: [] };
   119	  pairs.forEach(([label, title], i) => {
   120	    const id = res.ids[i];
   121	    if (id) {
   122	      if (!acc.byId.has(id)) { acc.byId.set(id, []); acc.idFiles.set(id, []); }
   123	      acc.byId.get(id).push(title);
   124	      acc.idFiles.get(id).push(label);
   125	    } else acc.unidentified.push([label, title]);
   126	  });
   127	  const sibSlice = res.ids.slice(pairs.length, pairs.length + siblingTitles.length);
   128	  const siblingIds = new Set(sibSlice.filter((x) => x !== null));
   129	  const integrityIds = res.ids.slice(pairs.length + siblingTitles.length);
   130	  return { acc, ids: res.ids.slice(0, pairs.length), siblingIds, integrityIds };
   131	}
   132	
   133	// Scenario titles a SIBLING active change genuinely declares — an EXEMPTION source, so it
   134	// is strict: each sibling delta must parse cleanly (parseDeltaStrict, zero problems, >0 ops)
   135	// and only scenarios inside its ADDED/MODIFIED block bodies count (a rename-only sibling's
   136	// scenarios already live in this change's projection and bind as out-of-scope reds).
   137	// Anomalies — malformed deltas, symlinks, escaping paths, unreadable files — grant NOTHING:
   138	// skipping is the fail-closed direction here (fewer exemptions = a stricter verdict); they
   139	// are never an ERROR, or a broken sibling could block every other change's gate.
   140	// fault-tolerant .md walk for the sibling scan ONLY: every readdir/lstat failure at any
   141	// depth yields zero entries from that branch — an anomaly must never throw out of verify()
   142	// nor create an infra ERROR (it merely grants no exemption)
   143	function safeMdWalk(dir) {
   144	  const out = [];
   145	  let entries;
   146	  try { entries = fs.readdirSync(dir, { withFileTypes: true }); } catch { return out; }
   147	  for (const e of entries) {
   148	    const p = path.join(dir, e.name);
   149	    try {
   150	      if (e.isDirectory()) out.push(...safeMdWalk(p));
   151	      else if (e.name.endsWith('.md')) out.push(p);
   152	    } catch { /* skip the branch */ }
   153	  }
   154	  return out.sort();
   155	}
   156	
   157	function collectSiblingTitles(cwd, change) {
   158	  const changesDir = path.resolve(cwd, 'apriori', 'changes');
   159	  const titles = [];
   160	  let entries = [];
   161	  try { entries = fs.readdirSync(changesDir, { withFileTypes: true }); } catch { return titles; }
   162	  for (const e of entries) {
   163	    if (!e.isDirectory() || e.name === change || e.name === 'archive') continue;
   164	    const siblingDir = path.join(changesDir, e.name);
   165	    if (!am.containsReal(changesDir, siblingDir)) continue;
   166	    const specsDir = path.join(siblingDir, 'specs');
   167	    let sd; try { sd = fs.lstatSync(specsDir); } catch { continue; }
   168	    if (sd.isSymbolicLink() || !sd.isDirectory() || !am.containsReal(siblingDir, specsDir)) continue;
   169	    for (const f of safeMdWalk(specsDir)) {
   170	      let st; try { st = fs.lstatSync(f); } catch { continue; }
   171	      if (st.isSymbolicLink() || !st.isFile() || !am.containsReal(specsDir, f)) continue;
   172	      let raw; try { raw = fs.readFileSync(f, 'utf8'); } catch { continue; }
   173	      const { delta, problems } = am.parseDeltaStrict(raw);
   174	      if (problems.length || am.deltaOpCount(delta) === 0) continue;   // malformed sibling grants nothing
   175	      const removed = new Set(delta.REMOVED.keys());
   176	      for (const bucket of [delta.ADDED, delta.MODIFIED])
   177	        for (const [name, block] of bucket) {
   178	          if (removed.has(name)) continue;
   179	          scanTitles(block, e.name, titles);
   180	        }
   181	    }
   182	  }
   183	  return titles.map(([, t]) => t);
   184	}

   186	// Batch-bind (label,title) pairs through a matcher → acc, or {failure} when the matcher fails.
   187	function bindPairs(pairs, matcher) {
   188	  const res = matcher.batch(pairs.map(([, title]) => title));
   189	  if (res.failure) return { failure: res.failure };
   190	  const acc = { byId: new Map(), idFiles: new Map(), unidentified: [] };
   191	  pairs.forEach(([label, title], i) => {
   192	    const id = res.ids[i];
   193	    if (id) {
   194	      if (!acc.byId.has(id)) { acc.byId.set(id, []); acc.idFiles.set(id, []); }
   195	      acc.byId.get(id).push(title);
   196	      acc.idFiles.get(id).push(label);
   197	    } else acc.unidentified.push([label, title]);
   198	  });
   199	  return acc;
   200	}

   202	function dupsOf(acc) {
   203	  return [...acc.byId.entries()].filter(([, titles]) => titles.length > 1)
   204	    .map(([id]) => ({ id, files: [...new Set(acc.idFiles.get(id))] }));
   205	}

   207	// Collect scenarios from spec files.
   208	// → { byId, unidentified:[[file,title]], files, missingTargets:[], duplicates:[{id,files}] }
   209	// (+ failure when the matcher's channel fails — callers fail closed on it)
   210	function collectScenarios(specTargets, idRe, matcher) {
   211	  const m = matcher || inlineMatcher(idRe);
   212	  const missingTargets = specTargets.filter((t) => !fs.existsSync(t));
   213	  const files = [...new Set(specTargets.flatMap(mdFiles))].sort();
   214	  const pairs = [];
   215	  for (const f of files) scanTitles(fs.readFileSync(f, 'utf8'), f, pairs);
   145	  const fails = [];
   146	  for (const [name, text] of Object.entries(files)) {
   147	    for (const tokens of codexCandidates(text)) {
   148	      if (!tokens.includes('resume')) continue;
   149	      if (CODEX_EXEMPT.some((e) => e.join(' ') === tokens.join(' '))) continue;
   150	      if (tokens.includes('-s')) fails.push(`${name}: resume command uses -s (rejected on codex >=0.14x): ${tokens.join(' ')}`);
   151	      if (!(tokens.includes('-c') && tokens.includes('sandbox_mode="read-only"')))
   152	        fails.push(`${name}: resume command missing -c sandbox_mode="read-only": ${tokens.join(' ')}`);
   153	    }
   154	  }
   155	  for (const rb of ['RUNBOOK.md', 'RUNBOOK_cn.md'])
   156	    if (files[rb] && !files[rb].includes('< /dev/null')) fails.push(`${rb}: missing the \`< /dev/null\` non-interactive guidance`);
   157	  return fails;
   158	}
   159	
   160	// ---- CK-04: every spec scenario carries a bindable ID -----------------------
   161	// Recognition goes through leadId — the same contract verify/gate/doctor use — never a
   162	// private `^(…)\b` anchoring that could disagree with them at the edges (CK-14).
   163	function checkScenarioIds(specText, name, idPattern = DEFAULT_ID) {
   164	  const idRe = new RegExp(idPattern);
   165	  const fails = [];
   166	  // fenced scenarios are documentation, not spec — same rule as the spec-runner (SR-13)
   167	  for (const m of stripFences(specText).matchAll(/^####\s+Scenario:\s+(.*)$/gm))
   168	    if (leadId(m[1].trim(), idRe) === null) fails.push(`${name}: scenario without a bindable ID: ${m[1].slice(0, 50)}`);
   169	  return fails;
   170	}
   171	
   172	// scenario titles of one spec text, in order (fence rule as above) — the CLI batches
   173	// these through the shared matcher so config-origin patterns stay terminable (CK-16)
   174	function scenarioTitles(specText) {
   175	  return [...stripFences(specText).matchAll(/^####\s+Scenario:\s+(.*)$/gm)].map((m) => m[1]);
   176	}
   177	
   178	// ---- CK-05: no OpenSpec adapter references remain (V3 is single plain-files) --
   179	const OPENSPEC_MARKERS = [/\/opsx:/, /openspec\//, /\(adapter:/i, /OpenSpec adapter/];
   180	function checkNoOpenspec(name, text) {
   181	  const fails = [];
   182	  for (const line of text.split('\n')) for (const re of OPENSPEC_MARKERS)
   183	    if (re.test(line)) { fails.push(`${name}: residual OpenSpec reference: ${line.trim().slice(0, 60)}`); break; }
   184	  return fails;
   185	}
   300	    // CK-04 over the spec store — a missing path is an ERROR, never a silent PASS
   301	    const specsPath = path.join(root, specsDir);
   302	    if (!fs.existsSync(specsPath)) {
   303	      console.error(`error: spec store path does not exist: ${specsDir}` +
   304	        (fs.existsSync(path.join(root, 'apriori')) ? '' : ` — is this an apriori project? (run 'apriori init')`));
   305	      console.log('\nRESULT: ERROR');
   306	      return 2;
   307	    }
   308	    const specFiles = [];
   309	    (function walk(d) {
   310	      for (const e of fs.readdirSync(d, { withFileTypes: true })) {
   311	        const p = path.join(d, e.name);
   312	        if (e.isDirectory()) walk(p); else if (e.name.endsWith('.md')) specFiles.push(p);
   313	      }
   314	    })(specsPath);
   315	    // CK-04 consumes the project's id-pattern (config row > default; check has no flag) and
   316	    // batches through the shared matcher — a broken or pathological row is RESULT: ERROR (CK-15/16)
   317	    const resolved = resolveIdPattern(root, null);
   318	    if (resolved.error) {
   319	      console.error('error: ' + resolved.error);
   320	      console.log('\nRESULT: ERROR');
   321	      return 2;
   322	    }
   323	    const pairs = [];
   324	    for (const f of specFiles) {
   325	      const rel = path.relative(root, f);
   326	      for (const t of scenarioTitles(fs.readFileSync(f, 'utf8'))) pairs.push([rel, t]);
   327	    }
   328	    const batch = makeIdMatcher(resolved).batch(pairs.map(([, t]) => t));
   329	    if (batch.failure) {
   330	      console.error('error: ' + matcherFailureMsg(batch.failure));   // the ONE sanitized exit (IMPL-3)
   331	      console.log('\nRESULT: ERROR');
   332	      return 2;
   333	    }
   334	    pairs.forEach(([rel, t], i) => {
   335	      if (batch.ids[i] === null) fails.push(`${rel}: scenario without a bindable ID: ${t.slice(0, 50)}`);
   336	    });
   337	
   338	    // CK-10: review-evidence secret tripwire (consumer mode; absent dir skips)
   339	    const secrets = checkReviewSecrets(root);
   340	    fails.push(...secrets.fails);
   341	    for (const w of secrets.warns) console.log('! ' + w);
   342	
   343	    if (self) {
   344	      // Handbook checks (apriori repo dogfooding) — run when the pairs exist.
   345	      // CK-08: a ONE-SIDED pair fails (a missing mirror must not skip silently);
   124	  // D6 is COMPUTED before the D5 probe (the id-pattern must be validated — and its store
   125	  // scan survived — before any test command runs; display order stays D5 then D6): an
   126	  // uncompilable or terminated config pattern skips the probe entirely.
   127	  const specsDir = path.join(ap, 'specs');
   128	  const d6Entries = [];
   129	  let idPatternBroken = false;
   130	  const idp = resolveIdPattern(cwd, null);
   131	  if (idp.error) {
   132	    idPatternBroken = true;
   133	    d6Entries.push({ id: 'D6', status: 'finding', detail: idp.error, fix: 'fix the id-pattern row in apriori/process-config.md' });
   134	  } else if (!fs.existsSync(specsDir)) d6Entries.push({ id: 'D6', status: 'n/a', detail: 'no store dir — see D2' });
   135	  else {
   136	    const collected = collectScenarios([specsDir], new RegExp(idp.source), makeIdMatcher(idp));
   137	    if (collected.failure) {
   138	      idPatternBroken = true;
   139	      d6Entries.push({ id: 'D6', status: 'finding', detail: matcherFailureMsg(collected.failure), fix: 'fix the id-pattern row in apriori/process-config.md' });
   140	    } else {
   141	      const { byId, unidentified, files, duplicates } = collected;
   142	      const src = ` (id-pattern: ${idp.origin})`;
   143	      if (!files.length || (byId.size === 0 && unidentified.length === 0))
   144	        d6Entries.push({ id: 'D6', status: 'n/a', detail: 'empty store — normal for a new project' });
   145	      else {
   146	        let bad = 0;
   147	        if (unidentified.length) { d6Entries.push({ id: 'D6', status: 'finding', detail: `scenario(s) without a bindable ${boundedSource(idp.source)} ID${src}: ${unidentified.map(([, t]) => t.slice(0, 40)).join(' · ')}`, fix: 'add leading IDs' }); bad++; }
   148	        if (duplicates.length) { d6Entries.push({ id: 'D6', status: 'finding', detail: `duplicate scenario IDs: ${duplicates.map((d) => d.id).join(', ')}`, fix: 'deduplicate IDs' }); bad++; }
   149	        if (!bad) d6Entries.push({ id: 'D6', status: 'ok', detail: `${byId.size} scenario(s), all bindable, no duplicates${src}` });
   150	      }
   151	    }
   152	  }
   153	
   154	  // D5 — TAP probe (the one declared side effect; --no-run and a broken id-pattern skip)
   155	  const cfgCmd = opts.testCmd ? null : configTestCmd(cwd);
   156	  const testCmd = opts.testCmd || (cfgCmd && cfgCmd.error ? null : cfgCmd);
   157	  if (idPatternBroken) checks.push({ id: 'D5', status: 'n/a', detail: 'probe skipped (invalid id-pattern config)' });
   158	  else if (cfgCmd && cfgCmd.error) checks.push({ id: 'D5', status: 'finding', detail: `config: ${cfgCmd.error}`, fix: 'keep one live test-cmd row' });
   159	  // --no-run is judged BEFORE the missing command: an explicit human skip is never a finding,
   160	  // and the missing-command branch below is one.
   161	  else if (opts.noRun) checks.push({ id: 'D5', status: 'n/a', detail: 'probe skipped (--no-run)' });
   162	  // A missing test command is not a neutral absence: it takes `apriori gate` with it. Reporting
   163	  // n/a here is what let a project run a whole change with a gate that never once ran.
   164	  else if (!testCmd) checks.push({ id: 'D5', status: 'finding',
   165	    detail: 'no test command configured — `apriori gate` cannot run C1 (the binding check) at all',
   166	    fix: 'add a test-cmd row to apriori/process-config.md (or pass --test-cmd)' });
   167	  else checks.push(classifyProbe(runTestCommand(testCmd, cwd)));
   168	
   169	  // D6 — store health (computed above, displayed here)
   170	  checks.push(...d6Entries);
   171	
   172	  // D7 — changes overview
   173	  const actives = activeChanges(cwd);
   174	  const notes = [];
   175	  let d7bad = 0;
   176	  const changesRoot = path.join(cwd, 'apriori', 'changes');
   177	  for (const c of actives) {
   178	    const fp = path.join(changesRoot, c, 'flow-state.md');
   179	    if (!fs.existsSync(fp)) { checks.push({ id: 'D7', status: 'finding', detail: `${c}: no flow-state.md`, fix: 'apriori new / restore the file' }); d7bad++; continue; }
   180	    // per-file containment: a symlinked flow-state pointing outside the changes tree is
     1	# TRUTH — archive-merge (`lib/archive-merge.js`, CLI subcommand `archive`)
     2	
     3	## Contract (code-is-truth)
     4	
     5	source-commit: 4127653
     6	
     7	**Responsibility.** Merge a change's delta spec into the living store by stable Requirement ID (STEP6 archive action); move the change dir to `changes/archive/`. Zero deps. 3.1: also the home of the merge-family pure helpers shared with `verify --change` — delta discovery, projection building, CAS base stamps, realpath containment — plus the high-level `archive --change` transaction and the `stamp` subcommand.
     8	
     9	**Delta format.** `## ADDED|MODIFIED|REMOVED|RENAMED Requirements` (h2 sections) containing `### Requirement: <name>` blocks (h3); RENAMED carries `- Old -> New` lines; optionally ONE CAS stamp line `<!-- apriori-base: sha256:<64hex> | new -->` before the first section. Parsing: `parseDeltaStrict(text)` → `{ delta, stamp, problems[] }` — since delta-consumption (3.3.x) a sequential fully-consuming line walker: every line outside opaque ``` fences belongs to exactly one legal construct (file preamble, legal section heading, section-preamble free text, requirement block, RENAMED rename line), and anything else — unrecognized h2 headings (one problem per heading, its lines attributed to NOTHING, stamps included), requirement/scenario markers outside their legal home, duplicate requirement names (within/across sections), and stamp problems (malformed/duplicate/late, matched in every non-skipped state) — is a `problems[]` entry carrying its 1-based line number; CRLF parses identically to LF; well-formed deltas parse byte-identically to the pre-walker grammar; `parseDelta(text)` = its delta only; `parseRequirementsStrict(text)` → `{ map, duplicates[] }` (first block wins, duplicates reported); `parseRequirements` = its map; `deltaOpCount(delta)` → total parsed ops (0 = no recognized section OR recognized sections with no parseable requirement/rename lines). `parseStamp(text)` → `{ stamp, problems }` — any standalone comment line ATTEMPTING apriori-base that fails the strict form is a problem, never silently 'unstamped'. `fingerprint(text)` → `sha256:<64hex>` over CRLF/CR→LF-normalized content.
    10	
    11	**Public interface.**
    12	- `merge(storeText, delta, change)` — PURE. → `{ store:Map, merged, modified, deprecated, renamed, conflicts, unchanged }`. Duplicate requirement names in the STORE text → conflicts (corruption guard). Order of application: RENAMED first (in-place key swap preserving document order), then ADDED, MODIFIED, REMOVED.
    13	  - ADDED on existing name: identical trimmed content → `unchanged` (idempotent rerun no-op) UNLESS the name was created by this run's RENAMED (`renamedTargets`) → conflict.
    14	  - MODIFIED replaces the whole block; missing target → conflict; delta block trim-equal to the store block → `unchanged` (the rerun/idempotence signature, since cas-enforcement).
    15	  - REMOVED keeps the block, injects `_deprecated (superseded by <change>)_` into the heading line.
    16	  - RENAMED with source gone + target present → `unchanged` ("already renamed" rerun signature); source gone + target absent → conflict; target already exists → conflict.
    17	  - REMOVED with target gone but `<name>  _deprecated (superseded by <THIS change>)_` present → `unchanged` ("already deprecated" rerun signature); deprecated by a DIFFERENT change → conflict.
    18	  - ANY conflict → nothing written (CLI exit 1).
    19	- `renderStore(storeText, store)` — preserves the store's header (text before the first `### Requirement:`), then blocks joined with `\n`.
    20	- `archiveStamp(d)` → `YYYY-MM-DDThhmm` (colon-free, CLI clock).
    21	- `CHANGE_NAME_RE` = `/^[a-z0-9]+(-[a-z0-9]+)*$/` (same rule as `apriori new`).
    22	- `archiveChangeDir(changesDir, name, now, ops?)` — validates name; realpath containment on BOTH source and destination (a symlinked `archive/` pointing outside is rejected before any move); `renameSync` (via ops when given) to `changes/archive/<stamp>-<name>/`; returns dest or null when source missing; throws on invalid name/escape.
    23	- `containsReal(root, target)` — realpath containment; not-yet-existing targets judged by nearest existing ancestor; STRICT (root itself is not inside root); fail-closed on unreadable paths.
    24	- `discoverDeltas(changesDirAbs, name)` → `{ files:[{abs,suffix}], errors[] }` — validates name (CHANGE_NAME_RE), change-dir existence + containment, specs/ itself containment-checked before becoming a root (symlinked specs/ rejected); walk follows symlink dirents explicitly (escaping links = errors, never silent skips). Errors are VALIDATION class → exit 2 on both surfaces.
    25	- `buildProjection(storeRoot, deltaFiles, change)` → `{ texts:Map<suffix,text>, modules, perModule, conflicts, casMismatches, hygiene, validation, unstampedMutations, notes, repaired }` — all store files pass through; each delta strict-parsed, CAS-checked (mismatch+all-ops-unchanged → repaired/noted instead of failed), merged in memory; nothing touches disk.
    26	- `archiveChange({cwd, change, changesDir, changesDirExplicit, write, ops})` → `{ code, out[], err[] }` — four phases, NO staging phase and NO post-commit writes of any kind (change-bundle, 4.0.0): the change dir is a complete bundle (requirement/ history, review/ evidence, gap-report.md, specs/ already inside), so the single atomic move carries everything; the command reads and writes nothing under any legacy root, is track-agnostic, and never deletes `spike/` (executor protocol, pre-archive). The four phases: preflight (discovery+hygiene+CAS+merges+pre-existing-temp guard+destination containment; any failure → nothing written), stage (`<store>.tmp-archive`, this-run cleanup on failure), commit (sorted per-file atomic renames; mid-commit failure → exact committed/not-committed/temps report, NO rollback), move (only changesDirExplicit, only after all commits). `ops` ({writeFileSync,renameSync,rmSync}) is the fault-injection seam.
    27	- `stampCli(argv)` — exactly one argv entry; absent file → `new` form; directory/unreadable → exit 2.
    28	- `cli(argv)`: high-level form `--change <name> [--write] [--changes-dir <dir>]` (no --store/--delta) → archiveChange; single-file form `--store <FILE> --delta <FILE> --change <name> [--write] [--changes-dir <dir>]` unchanged from 3.0.1 EXCEPT it now checks a present CAS stamp (mismatch → exit 1, nothing written) and rejects malformed deltas via parseDeltaStrict problems. Mixing --change with only one of --store/--delta → exit 2. Exit: 0 merged/no-op · 1 conflict/CAS/hygiene/zero-op/stage/commit/move-failure/pre-existing-temp · 2 usage/invalid name/missing change dir/containment.
    29	
    30	**Transactional write ordering (`--write`).** store staged to `<store>.tmp-archive` → dir move (atomic rename) → store committed via `renameSync(tmp, store)`. Move failure → tmp discarded, store byte-for-byte untouched. Limits: (a) tmp+rename gives single-file replace atomicity, NOT crash durability (no fsync on file or dir) — do not claim crash-durable; (b) the final `renameSync(tmp, store)` is NOT guarded — if it throws AFTER the change dir moved, the store is unchanged but the change dir is already archived and the tmp file is left behind (the "all or nothing" claim covers move failure, not store-commit failure after the move).
    31	
    32	**Three moments.** The store file is the shared state: init = created on first `--write`; update = full rewrite via temp+rename; cleanup = none (grows monotonically; REMOVED deprecates, never deletes).
    33	
    34	**Pitfalls.**
    35	- `merge` compares ADDED blocks with `.trim()` equality — whitespace-only differences count as conflicts, not no-ops.
    36	- `parseRequirementsStrict` keeps the FIRST duplicate block (previously last-wins) — visible only on invalid input, which now conflicts anyway.
    37	- `renderStore` drops anything BETWEEN requirement blocks that isn't part of a block (inter-block prose is not preserved — only the pre-first-block header is). Needs human confirmation whether any store relies on inter-block prose (repo's own stores do not).
    38	- CAS enforcement is GRADED since cas-enforcement (3.3.x): an unstamped delta STILL MERGES, but mutation-carrying ones (MODIFIED/REMOVED/RENAMED — ADDED-only exempt, the exemption is clobber-focused) warn on every surface (`unstampedWarning(suffix)`, both archive forms + verify --change stderr) and `buildProjection` returns `unstampedMutations: string[]` (suffixes) that gate C7 denies by default (--no-cas / `| cas | optional |` waive it, visibly). Stamps become mandatory in 4.0. Rerun repair: a stamped file whose base mismatches but whose EVERY op is `unchanged` (the already-applied signature; MODIFIED now reports `unchanged` on trim-equality) downgrades to a `rerun accepted` note in `notes[]`/`repaired[]` and contributes no writes — the move phase completes; a mismatch with ANY real pending op still fails the whole preflight.
    39	- Deprecation rewrites the heading LINE (`### Requirement: Name  _deprecated (superseded by x)_`), so on re-parse the requirement's map KEY includes the marker — MODIFIED/RENAMED can no longer address the block by its original name. (REMOVED reruns by the SAME change are now a no-op signature; see merge().) `DEPRECATED_RE`/`stripDeprecatedBlocks` exported — spec-runner uses them to exclude deprecated blocks from scenario collection.
    40	- CLI parsing via the shared strict parser (truth/args.md): --help exits 0; unknown flags/stray positionals exit 2; stampCli takes exactly one positional (a dash token is an unknown flag now, not the file).
    41	
    42	**buildProjection additions (verify-change-scope / modified-block-integrity, 2026-08-13).** The return additionally carries `deltaOps` (per-suffix operation buckets {added, modified, renamedPairs, removed} captured at THIS parse — the one delta snapshot; change-scope provenance never re-reads files) and `modifiedBlocks` (per-suffix raw {name, oldBlock, newBlock} pairs for every MODIFIED operation; rename-then-modify baselines the COMPLETE pre-rename block captured before the key swap, with a post-rename-name fallback so repaired reruns still owe their pair).
    43	
    44	**Modified-block integrity (modified-block-integrity, 2026-08-13).** Exports `compareModifiedBlock(oldBlock, newBlock, idOf)` — a PURE engine (zero fs, zero matcher; `idOf(title)→id|null` injected): trim-equal fast path (all-empty diff, no scan, no idOf); structure scan finds scenario delimiters on the fence-STRIPPED view with the verbatim state-A regex (`\s+` may cross newlines; inline/split headings valid; fenced lines within a cross-line delimiter span still join the body), requirement heading never compared; per-key cardinality truth table (retained/titleChanged mutually exclusive on 1:1; any side >1 → ambiguous {key, side, oldCount, newCount}, skipping every other class and body comparison); order-preserving greedy subsequence over {raw, norm} lines (match on normalized, report RAW originals; requirement-prose misses carry scenario=null). `formatIntegrityHuman(entries)` renders the shared `— MODIFIED INTEGRITY —` section (significance: any of dropped/missingLines/ambiguous/titleChanged prints the entry; fields C0/DEL→`·` then whole-line 119+ellipsis cap; empty → ''). The archive surface: `cli(argv, deps)` / `archiveChange({..., idMatcherFactory})` take an injected factory `(cwd)→matcher|{error}` (bin composes it from config.resolveIdPattern + spec-runner.makeIdMatcher — this module still never requires spec-runner); `pushIntegritySection` runs after EVERY preflight guard (temp/containment included) and before write-result lines (an output-order guarantee; archive output stays buffered); a missing/failing factory degrades to one stderr line `sanitizeMsg('warning: modified-integrity ' + reason)` with nothing else changed.
    45	
    46	## Decisions (doc-is-truth)
    47	
    48	- D-AM-a (active): the integrity report is INFORMATIVE by contract — it never changes a verdict, an exit code or written bytes, because a machine cannot distinguish an intended deletion from a slipped one; its job is to put the difference on the table for humans and the P8 review. Ratified in modified-block-integrity (2026-08-13).
    49	- D-AM-b (active): the archive-side id matcher arrives by dependency injection at the bin seam — archive-merge never requires spec-runner (the one-way dependency rule outranks convenience). Ratified in modified-block-integrity (2026-08-13).
    50	
    51	- D-AM-1 (active): same-ID conflict = stop, ledger issue, human resolves — the §4.11 serialize-per-module rule; the tool never auto-resolves.
    52	- D-AM-2 (superseded-by: D-AM-7): archive rerun idempotency as ratified in 3.0.1 covered two signatures — identical-content ADDED, and RENAMED source-gone+target-present.
    53	- D-AM-3 (active): a content-bearing delta parsing to zero operations errors instead of silently merging nothing.
    54	- D-AM-4 (active): REMOVED deprecates in place (audit trail), never physically deletes.
    55	- D-AM-5 (active): CAS base stamps are opt-in — §4.11's serialize rule tooling must not break unstamped pre-3.1 deltas. Ratified in change-projection (2026-07-10).
    56	- D-AM-6 (active): high-level archive guarantees failure-atomicity UP TO THE COMMIT POINT only; the commit phase is per-file atomic renames with an honest report on mid-commit failure and NO rollback (a rollback that half-fails is worse); crash durability (fsync) is explicitly NOT claimed (§4.8 wording discipline). Ratified in change-projection.
    57	- D-AM-7 (superseded-by: D-AM-8): rerun idempotency covered THREE signatures — identical-content ADDED; RENAMED source-gone+target-present; REMOVED already-deprecated-by-THIS-change. MODIFIED reruns re-applied. Supersedes D-AM-2. Ratified in change-projection.
    58	- D-AM-8 (active): rerun idempotency covers FOUR signatures — the D-AM-7 three plus MODIFIED trim-equality → `unchanged`. This is what makes the CAS rerun repair sound: a stamped delta whose base mismatches but whose every op hits a signature is already fully applied and re-runs cleanly (repaired suffixes contribute no write jobs, so they need no temp-ownership handling). Supersedes D-AM-7. Ratified in cas-enforcement.
    59	- D-AM-10 (active): unstamped mutation deltas are DENIED by default on both archive forms (preflight set, nothing written); waivers are visible and named (--no-cas wins over | cas | optional |). verify --change stays warn-only. Ratified in cas-mandatory (2026-07-12, GPT-5.6 review P0-2).
    60	- D-AM-9 (active): the atomic move carries the whole bundle — no staging phase, no post-commit writes; the 3.4.x requirement-staging phase (req-sweep) is deleted because the bundle already contains its history. `spike/` deletion is executor protocol pre-archive; the command never touches it. Ratified in change-bundle (2026-07-12).
     1	# TRUTH — doctor (`lib/doctor.js`, CLI subcommand `doctor`)
     2	
     3	## Contract (code-is-truth)
     4	
     5	source-commit: 3d32d6f
     6	
     7	**Responsibility.** Diagnose the project↔apriori seam with EIGHT checks (D1..D8); one exit code: 0 HEALTHY · 1 FINDINGS · 2 UNUSABLE (Node < 22, `apriori/` missing/not-a-dir, positional-arg usage error — each still listing the checks that ran). Never repairs, never writes; the ONLY side effect is D5 running the test command once (`--no-run` removes it). Findings carry a `fix` naming the repair command.
     8	
     9	**Public interface.**
    10	- `runDoctor({cwd, testCmd, noRun, nodeVersion})` → `{ code, result: 'HEALTHY'|'FINDINGS'|'UNUSABLE', findings, checks: [{id, status: 'ok'|'finding'|'n/a', detail, fix?}], errors }`. `checks` may carry MULTIPLE entries per D-id (D2 one per gap, D4 one per broken tool); `findings` = count of finding-status entries. `nodeVersion` is the DR-12 test seam.
    11	- `classifyProbe(run)` — D5's classifier over `runTestCommand`'s shape `{out, status, signal, error}`; ladder: spawn error → signal → bailout → empty output → zeroTapParsed (non-TAP) → unexplained non-zero exit (BEFORE any ok branch — a non-zero `1..0` lands here) → zero parsed results (`1..0` ok, else truncated/malformed) → ok with p/f counts (test failures are NOT findings).
    12	- `cli(argv)`: `[--test-cmd "<cmd>"] [--no-run] [--cwd <dir>] [--json]` via the shared strict parser (truth/args.md); any positional or unknown flag → exit 2 (pure JSON under `--json` via the jsonError seam); `--json` shape `{result, findings, checks, errors}` in every class.
    13	
    14	**The eight checks.** D1 Node ≥ 22 (injectable) · D2 scaffold with TYPE-HONEST probes (isDir/isFile/readIfFile — a file impersonating specs/ or tmp/, a dir at runbook.md/.gitignore, unreadable entries → findings, never crashes; missing process-config = n/a note) · D3 freshness via `checkRunbookFreshness`, gated on `isFile(runbook)` (absence/wrong type → n/a "see D2") · D4 pointers for `detectTools` hits only (rules file regular+contains `apriori/runbook.md`; command-level file exists) · D5 probe (above), whose branch order is: invalid id-pattern → `n/a` · config problem (conflicting/unreadable `test-cmd` row) → finding · `--no-run` → `n/a` · NO test command configured → **finding** naming the consequence (`apriori gate` cannot run C1 at all) with the `process-config.md` cure · else run the probe. `--no-run` deliberately sorts AHEAD of the missing-command branch — an explicit human skip is never a defect · D6 store via `collectScenarios` default pattern (missing dir → n/a "see D2"; empty → n/a; unidentified/dupes → findings) · D7 active flow-states (exists + per-file `containsReal` before ANY read — a symlinked flow-state is diagnosed, never read + non-empty `change` + matches dir) + archived stamp-dirs (directories only, `containsReal(archRoot,…)` before ANY read; escaping symlink → skip note) with not-DONE/ABANDONED surfaced as "gate ④ possibly pending" info.
    15	
    16	**Pitfalls.**
    17	- Requires ./init, ./check, ./spec-runner, ./status, ./archive-merge (leaf module; nothing requires doctor).
    18	- D5's runtime = the project's test command runtime.
    19	- On the apriori source repo itself, doctor honestly reports FINDINGS (no runbook copy — the repo is not an initialized consumer); that is correct, not a bug.
    20	
    21	**id-pattern (gate-id-pattern, 2026-08-13).** D6 scans the store with the effective id-pattern (config row > DEFAULT_ID; no flag) and its detail names the source (`(id-pattern: config)` / `(id-pattern: default)`); the unidentified finding echoes the actual source bounded. The pattern is resolved and its D6 scan EXECUTED before the D5 probe (execution order; display order unchanged): an uncompilable row — or a config-origin scan whose terminable child fails — makes D6 a finding naming process-config, and D5 becomes `n/a` `probe skipped (invalid id-pattern config)` with the test command never spawned. D5's own TAP parsing keeps DEFAULT_ID (its parsed-count classification is pattern-insensitive).
    22	
    23	## Decisions (doc-is-truth)
    24	
    25	- D-DR-b (active): a missing test command is a FINDING, not a neutral `n/a`. It takes `apriori gate`'s C1 with it, and reporting `n/a` made doctor print HEALTHY about a project whose gate was dead — the mechanism that let a real brownfield change run its whole life with zero gate runs. A docs-only project will therefore carry one standing D5 finding; that finding states a true fact (its gate C1 is unrunnable), and a declarative `test-cmd: none` opt-out is deliberately left to a future change. Ratified in gate-degrades (2026-08-14).
    26	
    27	- D-DR-a (active): a broken id-pattern config is a FINDING (exit 1), not UNUSABLE — doctor diagnoses repairable configs; but it still skips the probe, because no test command may run under a broken id-pattern. Ratified in gate-id-pattern (2026-08-13).
    28	
    29	- D-DR-1 (active): test failures are NOT doctor findings — D5 probes plumbing (does the command speak TAP), not correctness; red tests are `verify`'s business. Ratified in doctor-command (2026-07-11).
    30	- D-DR-2 (active): gate-④-pending archived changes are information, never findings — that state is the §4 archive sequencing working as designed.
    31	- D-DR-3 (active): doctor diagnoses and points at the fixer; it never repairs — repair actions belong to `init`/`update`/the human.
    32	- D-DR-4 (active): wrong-type scaffold entries (file-as-dir etc.) are findings, never false passes or crashes — malformed scaffolds are doctor's core case.

   210	function collectScenarios(specTargets, idRe, matcher) {
   211	  const m = matcher || inlineMatcher(idRe);
   212	  const missingTargets = specTargets.filter((t) => !fs.existsSync(t));
   213	  const files = [...new Set(specTargets.flatMap(mdFiles))].sort();
   214	  const pairs = [];
   215	  for (const f of files) scanTitles(fs.readFileSync(f, 'utf8'), f, pairs);
   216	  const acc = bindPairs(pairs, m);
   217	  if (acc.failure) return { failure: acc.failure, byId: new Map(), unidentified: [], files, missingTargets, duplicates: [] };
   218	  return { byId: acc.byId, unidentified: acc.unidentified, files, missingTargets, duplicates: dupsOf(acc) };
   219	}

   221	// Same collection over in-memory texts (the projection path) — label = store-relative suffix.
   222	function collectScenariosFromTexts(texts, idRe, matcher) {
   223	  const m = matcher || inlineMatcher(idRe);
   224	  const pairs = [];
   225	  for (const [label, text] of texts) scanTitles(text, label, pairs);
   226	  const acc = bindPairs(pairs, m);
   227	  if (acc.failure) return { failure: acc.failure, byId: new Map(), unidentified: [], files: [...texts.keys()], missingTargets: [], duplicates: [] };
   228	  return { byId: acc.byId, unidentified: acc.unidentified, files: [...texts.keys()], missingTargets: [], duplicates: dupsOf(acc) };
   229	}
